M^{fact}_{\tau}({v_{i}}w_{i}^{T})+M^{fact}_{{\tau}^{T}}({w_{i}}v_{i}^{T})\preceq B_{norm}(\tau)\left(M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\tau}}}({v_{i}}v_{i}^{T})+M^{fact}_{Id_{V_{\tau}}}({w_{i}}w_{i}^{T})\right)

over all
i
.

Theorem
8.2
now follows directly. For all
U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}
and all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}
, using Corollary
8.6
with
H_{U}=H_{V}=\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}
,

M^{fact}_{\tau}(H_{\tau})+M^{fact}_{{\tau}^{T}}(H_{\tau^{T}})\preceq\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})+\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})

Summing this equation over all
U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}
and all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}
, we obtain that

\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{M^{fact}_{\tau}(H_{\tau})}}\preceq 2\varepsilon^{\prime}\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})}

as needed.

8.2
Intersection Term Analysis Strategy

As we saw in the previous subsection, the analysis works out nicely if we work with
M^{fact}
. Unfortunately, our matrices are expressed in terms of
M^{orth}
. In this subsection, we describe our strategy for analyzing the difference between
M^{fact}
and
M^{orth}
.

Recall the following expressions for
\left(M^{fact}_{\tau}(H)\right)(A,B)
and
\left(M^{orth}_{\tau}(H)\right)(A,B)
where
A
has shape
U_{\tau}
and
B
has shape
V_{\tau}
:

\left(M^{fact}_{\tau}(H)\right)(A,B)=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{L}_{U_{\tau}},\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{V_{\tau}}}{H(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\sum_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}}{\sum_{R_{1}\in\mathcal{R}(\sigma,A,A^{\prime}),R_{2}\in\mathcal{R}(\tau,A^{\prime},B^{\prime}),\atop R_{3}\in\mathcal{R}({\sigma^{\prime}}^{T},B^{\prime},B)}M_{R_{1}}(A,A^{\prime})M_{R_{2}}(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})M_{R_{3}}(B^{\prime},B)}}

\displaystyle\left(M^{orth}_{\tau}(H)\right)(A,B)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{L}_{U_{\tau}},\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{V_{\tau}}}{H(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\sum_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}}{\sum_{R_{1}\in\mathcal{R}(\sigma,A,A^{\prime}),R_{2}\in\mathcal{R}(\tau,A^{\prime},B^{\prime}),\atop{R_{3}\in\mathcal{R}({\sigma^{\prime}}^{T},B^{\prime},B),R_{1},R_{2},R_{3}\text{ are properly composable}}}M_{R_{1}}(A,A^{\prime})M_{R_{2}}(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})M_{R_{3}}(B^{\prime},B)}}

This implies that
\left(M^{fact}_{\tau}(H)\right)(A,B)-\left(M^{orth}_{\tau}(H)\right)(A,B)
is equal to

\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{L}_{U_{\tau}},\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{V_{\tau}}}{H(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\sum_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}}{\sum_{R_{1}\in\mathcal{R}(\sigma,A,A^{\prime}),R_{2}\in\mathcal{R}(\tau,A^{\prime},B^{\prime}),\text{ and }R_{3}\in\mathcal{R}({\sigma^{\prime}}^{T},B^{\prime},B)\atop R_{1},R_{2},R_{3}\text{ are not properly composable}}M_{R_{1}}(A,A^{\prime})M_{R_{2}}(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})M_{R_{3}}(B^{\prime},B)}}

Thus, to understand the difference between
M^{fact}
and
M^{orth}
, we need to analyze the terms
\chi_{R_{1}}\chi_{R_{2}}\chi_{R_{3}}=\chi_{R_{1}\circ R_{2}\circ R_{3}}
for ribbons
R_{1},R_{2},R_{3}
which are composable but not properly composable. These terms, which we call intersection terms, are not negligible and must be analyzed carefully. In particular, we decompose each resulting ribbon
R=R_{1}\circ R_{2}\circ R_{3}
into new left, middle, and right parts. We do this as follows:

1.

Let
V_{*}
be the set of vertices which appear more than once in
V(R_{1}\circ R_{2}\circ R_{3})
. In other words,
V_{*}
is the set of vertices involved in the intersections between
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
, and
R_{3}
(not counting the facts that
B_{R_{1}}=A_{R_{2}}
and
B_{R_{2}}=A_{R_{3}}
because we expect these intersections).

2.

Let
A^{\prime}
be the leftmost minimum vertex separator of
A_{R_{1}}
and
B_{R_{1}}\cup V_{*}
in
R_{1}
. We turn
A^{\prime}
into a matrix index by specifying an ordering
O_{A^{\prime}}
for the vertices in
A^{\prime}
.

3.

Let
B^{\prime}
be the leftmost minimum vertex separator of
A_{R_{3}}\cup V_{*}
and
B_{R_{3}}
in
R_{2}
. We turn
B^{\prime}
into a matrix index by specifying an ordering
O_{B^{\prime}}
for the vertices in
B^{\prime}
.

4.

Decompose
R_{1}
as
R_{1}={R^{\prime}}_{1}\cup R_{4}
where
{R^{\prime}}_{1}
is the part of
R_{1}
between
A_{R_{1}}
and
A^{\prime}
and
R_{4}
is the part of
R_{1}
between
B^{\prime}
and
B_{R_{1}}=A_{R_{2}}
. Similarly, decompose

R_{3}
as
R_{3}=R_{5}\cup{R^{\prime}}_{3}
where
R_{5}
is the part of
R_{3}
between
B_{R_{1}}=A_{R_{2}}
and
B^{\prime}
and
{R^{\prime}}_{3}
is the part of
R_{3}
between
B^{\prime}
and
B_{R_{3}}
.

5.

Take
R^{\prime}_{2}=R_{4}\circ R_{2}\circ R_{5}
and note that
R^{\prime}_{1}\circ R^{\prime}_{2}\circ R^{\prime}_{3}=R_{1}\circ R_{2}\circ R_{3}
. We view
R^{\prime}_{1},R^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{3}
as the left, middle, and right parts of
R=R_{1}\circ R_{2}\circ R_{3}

While we will verify our analysis by checking the coefficients of the ribbons, we want to express everything in terms of shapes. We use the following conventions for the names of the shapes:

1.

As usual, we let
\sigma
,
\tau
, and
{\sigma^{\prime}}^{T}
be the shapes of
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
, and
R_{3}
.

2.

We let
\gamma
and
{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
be the shapes of
R_{4}
and
R_{5}
.

3.

We let
\sigma_{2}
,
\tau_{P}
, and
{\sigma^{\prime}_{2}}^{T}
be the shapes of
R^{\prime}_{1}
,
R^{\prime}_{2}
, and
R^{\prime}_{3}
. Here
P
is the intersection pattern induced by
R_{4}
,
R_{2}
, and
R_{5}
which we define in the next subsection.

Remark 8.7
.

A key feature of our analysis is that it will work the same way regardless of the shapes
\sigma_{2},{\sigma^{\prime}_{2}}^{T}
of
R^{\prime}_{1}
and
R^{\prime}_{3}
. In other words, if we replace
\sigma_{2}
by
\sigma_{2a}
and
\sigma^{\prime}_{2}
by
\sigma^{\prime}_{2a}
for a given intersection term, this just replaces
\sigma=\sigma_{2}\cup\gamma
with
\sigma_{a}=\sigma_{2a}\cup\gamma
and
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}_{2}\cup\gamma^{\prime}
with
\sigma^{\prime}_{a}=\sigma^{\prime}_{2a}\cup\gamma^{\prime}
. This allows us to focus on the shapes
\gamma
,
\tau
, and
{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
and is the reason why the
-\gamma,\gamma
operation appears in our results.

8.3
Intersection Term Analysis

In this section, we implement our strategy for analyzing intersection terms. For simplicity, we only give rough definitions and proof sketches here. For a more rigorous treatment, see Apendix
B
.

We begin by defining intersection patterns which describe how the ribbons
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
, and
R_{3}
intersect.

Definition 8.8

(Rough Definition of Intersection Patterns)
.

Given
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\}
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}
, and ribbons
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
, and
R_{3}
of shapes
\gamma
,
\tau
, and
{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
which are composable but not properly composable, we define the intersection pattern
P
induced by
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
, and
R_{3}
and the resulting shape
\tau_{P}
as follows:

1.

We take
V(P)=V(\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})
.

2.

We take
E(P)
to be the set of edges
(u,v)
such that
u,v
are distinct vertices in
V(\sigma\circ\tau\circ{\sigma^{\prime}}^{T})
but
u
and
v
correspond to the same vertex in
R_{1}\circ R_{2}\circ R_{3}

3.

We define
\tau_{P}
to be the shape of the ribbon
R=R_{1}\circ R_{2}\circ R_{3}

Definition 8.9
.

Given
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\}
, and
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}
, we define
\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
to be the set of all possible intersection patterns
P
which can be induced by ribbons
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
, and
R_{3}
of shapes
\gamma
,
\tau
, and
{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
.

Remark 8.10
.

Note that if
\gamma=Id_{U_{\tau}}
and
\gamma^{\prime}=Id_{V_{\tau}}
then
\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}=\emptyset
as every intersection pattern must have an unexpected intersection so either
\gamma
or
\gamma^{\prime}
must be non-trivial.

It would be nice if the intersection pattern
P
together with the ribbon
R
allowed us to recover the original ribbons
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
, and
R_{3}
. Unfortunately, it is possible for different triples of ribbons to result in the same intersection pattern
P
and ribbon
R
. That said, the number of such triples cannot be too large, and this is sufficient for our purposes.

Definition 8.11
.

Given an intersection pattern
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
, let
R
be a ribbon of shape
\tau_{P}
. We define
N(P)
to be the number of different triples of ribbons
R_{1},R_{2},R_{3}
such that
R_{1}\circ R_{2}\circ R_{3}=R
and
R_{1},R_{2},R_{3}
induce the intersection pattern
P
.

Lemma 8.12
.

For all intersection patterns
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
,
N(P)\leq|V(\tau_{P})|^{|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus U_{\gamma^{\prime}}|}

Proof sketch.

This can be proved by making the following observations:

1.

A_{R_{1}}=A_{R}
and
B_{R_{3}}=B_{R}
.

2.

All of the remining vertices in
V(R_{1})
and
V(R_{3})
must be equal to some vertex in
V(R)
.

3.

Once
R_{1}
and
R_{3}
are determined, there is at most one ribbon
R_{2}
such that
R_{1},R_{2},R_{3}
are composable,
R=R_{1}\circ R_{2}\circ R_{3}
, and
R_{1},R_{2},R_{3}
induce the intersection pattern
P
.

With these definitions, we can now analyze the intersection terms.

Definition 8.13
.

Given a left shape
\sigma
, define
e_{\sigma}
to be the vector which has a
1
in coordinate
\sigma
and has a
0
in all other coordinates.

Lemma 8.14
.

For all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\sigma\in\mathcal{L}_{U_{\tau}}
, and
\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{V_{\tau}}
,

\displaystyle M^{fact}_{\tau}(e_{\sigma}e^{T}_{\sigma^{\prime}})-M^{orth}_{\tau}(e_{\sigma}e^{T}_{\sigma^{\prime}})=\sum_{\sigma_{2}\in\mathcal{L},\gamma\in\Gamma:\sigma_{2}\circ\gamma=\sigma}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|}\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,Id_{V_{\tau}}}}N(P)M^{orth}_{\tau_{P}}(e_{\sigma_{2}}e^{T}_{\sigma^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{L},\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma:\sigma^{\prime}_{2}\circ\gamma^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|}\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{Id_{U_{\tau}},\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}}N(P)M^{orth}_{\tau_{P}}(e_{\sigma}e^{T}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{2}})}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\sigma_{2}\in\mathcal{L},\gamma\in\Gamma:\sigma_{2}\circ\gamma=\sigma}{\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{L},\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma:\sigma^{\prime}_{2}\circ\gamma^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|\cdot|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|}\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}}N(P)M^{orth}_{\tau_{P}}(e_{\sigma_{2}}e^{T}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{2}})}}

Proof sketch.

This lemma follows from the following bijection. Consider the third term

\sum_{\sigma_{2}\in\mathcal{L},\gamma\in\Gamma:\sigma_{2}\circ\gamma=\sigma}{\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{L},\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma:\sigma^{\prime}_{2}\circ\gamma^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|\cdot|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|}\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}}N(P)M^{orth}_{\tau_{P}}(e_{\sigma_{2}}e^{T}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{2}})}}

On one side, we have the following data:

1.

Ribbons
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
, and
R_{3}
of shapes
\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
such that
R_{1},R_{2},R_{3}
are composable but
R_{1}
and
R_{2}\circ R_{3}
are not properly composable (i.e.
R_{1}
has an unexpected intersection with
R_{2}
and/or
R_{3}
) and
R_{1}\circ R_{2}
and
R_{3}
are not properly composable (i.e.
R_{3}
has an unexpected intersection with
R_{1}
and/or
R_{2}
).

2.

An ordering
O_{A^{\prime}}
on the leftmost minimum vertex separator
A^{\prime}
of
A_{R_{1}}
and
V_{*}\cup B_{R_{1}}
(recall that
V_{*}
is the set of vertices which appear more than once in
V(R_{1}\circ R_{2}\circ R_{3})
).

3.

An ordering
O_{B^{\prime}}
on the rightmost minimum vertex separator
B^{\prime}
of
V_{*}\cup A_{R_{3}}
and
B_{R_{3}}
.

On the other side, we have the following data

1.

An intersection pattern
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
where
\gamma
and
{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
are non-trivial.

2.

Ribbons
R^{\prime}_{1}
,
R^{\prime}_{2}
,
R^{\prime}_{3}
of shapes
\sigma_{2}
,
\tau_{P}
,
{\sigma^{\prime}_{2}}^{T}
which are properly composable

3.

A number in
[N(P)]
describing which possible triple of ribbons resulted in the intersection pattern
P
and the ribbon
R^{\prime}_{2}
.

To see this bijection, note that given the data on the first side, we can recover the ribbons
R^{\prime}_{1}
,
R^{\prime}_{2}
, and
R^{\prime}_{3}
as follows:

1.

We decompose
R_{1}
as
R_{1}=R^{\prime}_{1}\circ R_{4}
where
B_{R^{\prime}_{1}}=A_{R_{4}}=A^{\prime}
with the ordering
O_{A^{\prime}}
.

2.

We decompose
R_{3}
as
R_{3}=R_{5}\circ R^{\prime}_{3}
where where
B_{R_{5}}=A_{R^{\prime}_{3}}=B^{\prime}
with the ordering
O_{B^{\prime}}
.

3.

We take
R^{\prime}_{2}=R_{4}\circ R_{2}\circ R_{5}
.

The intersection pattern
P
and the number in
[N(P)]
can be obtained from
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
, and
R_{3}
.

Conversely, with the data on the other side, we can recover the data on the first side as follows:

1.

R^{\prime}_{2}
gives an ordering
O_{A^{\prime}}
for
A^{\prime}=A_{R^{\prime}_{2}}
and an ordering
O_{B^{\prime}}
for
B^{\prime}=B_{R^{\prime}_{2}}
.

2.

The ribbon
R^{\prime}_{2}
, intersection pattern
P
, and number in
[N(P)]
allow us to recover
R_{4}
,
R_{2}
, and
R_{5}
.

3.

We take
R_{1}=R^{\prime}_{1}\circ R_{4}
and
R_{3}=R_{5}\circ R^{\prime}_{3}
.

Thus, both sides have the same coefficient for each ribbon.

The analysis for the the first term is the same except that when
\gamma^{\prime}
is trivial, we always take
\gamma^{\prime}=Id_{V_{\tau}}
. Thus, we always have that
B^{\prime}=B_{R^{\prime}_{2}}=B_{R_{2}}
(with the same ordering) and
R^{\prime}_{3}=R_{3}=Id_{B^{\prime}}
. Because of this, there is no need to specify
R_{3}
,
R^{\prime}_{3}
,
R_{5}
, or an ordering on
B^{\prime}
.

Similarly, the analysis for the the second term is the same except that when
\gamma
is trivial, we always take
\gamma=Id_{U_{\tau}}
. Thus, we always have that
A^{\prime}=A_{R^{\prime}_{2}}=A_{R_{2}}
(with the same ordering) and
R^{\prime}_{1}=R_{1}=Id_{A^{\prime}}
. Because of this, there is no need to specify
R_{1}
,
R^{\prime}_{1}
,
R_{4}
, or an ordering on
A^{\prime}
.

Applying Lemma
8.14
for all
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
simultaneously, we obtain the following corollary.

Definition 8.15
.

For all
U,V\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}
, given a
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,V}
and a vector
v
indexed by left shapes
\sigma\in\mathcal{L}_{V}
, define
v^{-\gamma}
to be the vector indexed by left shapes
\sigma_{2}\in\mathcal{L}_{U}
such that
v^{-\gamma}(\sigma_{2})=v(\sigma_{2}\circ\gamma)
if
\sigma_{2}\circ\gamma\in\mathcal{L}_{V}
and
v^{-\gamma}(\sigma_{2})=0
otherwise.

Proposition 8.16
.

For all composable
\gamma_{2},\gamma_{1}\in\Gamma
and all vectors
v
indexed by left shapes in
\mathcal{L}_{V_{\gamma_{1}}}
,
(v^{-\gamma_{1}})^{-\gamma_{2}}=v^{-\gamma_{2}\circ\gamma_{1}}

Corollary 8.17
.

For all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, for all left
\tau
-vectors
v
and all right
\tau
-vectors
w
,

\displaystyle M^{orth}_{\tau}(vw^{T})=M^{fact}_{\tau}(vw^{T})-\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|}\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,Id_{V_{\tau}}}}N(P)M^{orth}_{\tau_{P}}(v^{-\gamma}w^{T})}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|}\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{Id_{U_{\tau}},\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}}N(P)M^{orth}_{\tau_{P}}(v(w^{-\gamma})^{T})}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}}{\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|\cdot|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|}\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}}N(P)M^{orth}_{\tau_{P}}(v^{-\gamma}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}})^{T})}}

Applying Corollary
8.17
iteratively, we obtain the following theorem:

Definition 8.18
.

Given
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma\cup\{Id_{U}:U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}\}
and
j>0
, let
\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}
be the set of all
\gamma_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma\cup\{Id_{U}:U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}\}
such that:

1.

\gamma_{j},\ldots,\gamma_{1}
are composable and
\gamma_{j}\circ\ldots\circ\gamma_{1}=\gamma

2.

\gamma^{\prime}_{j},\ldots,\gamma^{\prime}_{1}
are composable and
\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\circ\ldots\circ\gamma^{\prime}_{1}=\gamma^{\prime}

3.

For all
i\in[1,j]
,
\gamma_{i}
or
\gamma^{\prime}_{i}
is non-trivial (i.e.
\gamma_{i}\neq Id_{U_{\gamma_{i}}}
or
\gamma^{\prime}_{i}\neq Id_{U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}}
).

Remark 8.19
.

Note that if
\gamma=Id_{U}
and
\gamma^{\prime}=Id_{V}
then for all
j>0
,
\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}=\emptyset
.

Theorem 8.20
.

For all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, left
\tau
-vectors
v
, and right
\tau
-vectors
w
,

\displaystyle M^{orth}_{\tau}(v{w^{T}})=M^{fact}_{\tau}(v{w^{T}})+

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\},\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}:\atop\gamma\text{ or }\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial }}\sum_{j>0}{(-1)^{j}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}}{\prod_{i:\gamma_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma_{i}})|}}\prod_{i:\gamma^{\prime}_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}})|}}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{P_{1},\cdots,P_{j}:P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j}{N(P_{i})}\right)M^{fact}_{\tau_{P_{j}}}(v^{-\gamma}{(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}})^{T}})}

where we take
\tau_{P_{0}}=\tau
.

8.4
Bounding the difference between
M^{fact}
and
M^{orth}

In this subsection, we bound the difference between
M^{fact}_{\tau}(H_{\tau})
and
M^{orth}_{\tau}(H_{\tau})
. We recall the following conditions on
B(\gamma)
,
N(\gamma)
, and
c(\gamma)
:

1.

For all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\}
, and
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j>0}{\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}}{\left(\prod_{i:\gamma_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma_{i}})|}}\right)\left(\prod_{i:\gamma^{\prime}_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}})|}}\right)}}

\displaystyle\sum_{P_{1},\cdots,P_{j}:P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j}{N(P_{i})}\right)}\leq\frac{N(\gamma)N(\gamma^{\prime})}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}}

2.

For all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}
, and
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}
, for all
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
,

B_{norm}(\tau_{P})\leq B(\gamma)B(\gamma^{\prime})B_{norm}(\tau)

3.

\forall V\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,V}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|c(\gamma)}}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{20}

With these conditions, we can now bound the difference between
M^{fact}
and
M^{orth}
.

Lemma 8.21
.

If the norm bounds and the conditions on
B(\gamma)
,
N(\gamma)
, and
c(\gamma)
hold then for all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, left
\tau
-vectors
v
, and right
\tau
-vectors
w
,

\displaystyle\left(M^{fact}_{\tau}(v{w^{T}})+M^{fact}_{{\tau}^{T}}(w{v^{T}})\right)-\left(M^{orth}_{\tau}(v{w^{T}})+M^{orth}_{{\tau}^{T}}(w{v^{T}})\right)\preceq

\displaystyle{\varepsilon^{\prime}}B_{norm}(\tau)M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\tau}}}(vv^{T})+2\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}}{\frac{B(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B_{norm}(\tau){c(\gamma)}}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(v^{-\gamma}(v^{-\gamma})^{T})}+

\displaystyle{\varepsilon^{\prime}}B_{norm}(\tau)M^{fact}_{Id_{V_{\tau}}}(ww^{T})+2\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}}{\frac{B(\gamma^{\prime})^{2}N(\gamma^{\prime})^{2}B_{norm}(\tau){c(\gamma^{\prime})}}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma^{\prime}}}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}})^{T})}

Proof.

By Theorem
8.20
, taking
\tau_{P_{0}}=\tau
,

\displaystyle M^{orth}_{\tau}(v{w^{T}})=M^{fact}_{\tau}(v{w^{T}})+

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\},\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}:\atop\gamma\text{ or }\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial }}\sum_{j>0}{(-1)^{j}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}}{\prod_{i:\gamma_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma_{i}})|}}\prod_{i:\gamma^{\prime}_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}})|}}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{P_{1},\cdots,P_{j}:P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j}{N(P_{i})}\right)M^{fact}_{\tau_{P_{j}}}(v^{-\gamma}{(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}})^{T}})}

Taking the transpose of this equation gives

\displaystyle M^{orth}_{{\tau}^{T}}(w{v^{T}})=M^{fact}_{{\tau}^{T}}(w{v^{T}})+

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\},\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}:\atop\gamma\text{ or }\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial }}\sum_{j>0}{(-1)^{j}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}}{\prod_{i:\gamma_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma_{i}})|}}\prod_{i:\gamma^{\prime}_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}})|}}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{P_{1},\cdots,P_{j}:P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j}{N(P_{i})}\right)M^{fact}_{\tau^{T}_{P_{j}}}({w^{-\gamma^{\prime}}(v^{-\gamma})^{T}})}

Now observe that by Lemma
8.5
, if the norm bounds hold,

\displaystyle{\pm}\left(M^{fact}_{\tau_{P_{j}}}(v^{-\gamma}{(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}})^{T}})+M^{fact}_{\tau^{T}_{P_{j}}}({w^{-\gamma^{\prime}}(v^{-\gamma})^{T}})\right)=

\displaystyle{\pm}M^{fact}_{\tau_{P_{j}}}\left(\left(\sqrt{\frac{N(\gamma)B(\gamma){c(\gamma)}}{N(\gamma^{\prime})B(\gamma^{\prime}){c(\gamma^{\prime})}}}v^{-\gamma}\right)\left(\sqrt{\frac{N(\gamma^{\prime})B(\gamma^{\prime}){c(\gamma^{\prime})}}{N(\gamma)B(\gamma){c(\gamma)}}}{(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}})^{T}}\right)\right)\pm

\displaystyle M^{fact}_{\tau^{T}_{P_{j}}}\left(\left(\sqrt{\frac{N(\gamma^{\prime})B(\gamma^{\prime}){c(\gamma^{\prime})}}{N(\gamma)B(\gamma){c(\gamma)}}}w^{-\gamma^{\prime}}\right)\left(\sqrt{\frac{N(\gamma)B(\gamma){c(\gamma)}}{N(\gamma^{\prime})B(\gamma^{\prime}){c(\gamma^{\prime})}}}{(v^{-\gamma})^{T}}\right)\right)\preceq

\displaystyle B_{norm}(\tau_{P_{j}})\left(\frac{N(\gamma)B(\gamma){c(\gamma)}}{N(\gamma^{\prime})B(\gamma^{\prime}){c(\gamma^{\prime})}}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(v^{-\gamma}(v^{-\gamma})^{T})+\frac{N(\gamma^{\prime})B(\gamma^{\prime}){c(\gamma^{\prime})}}{N(\gamma)B(\gamma){c(\gamma)}}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma^{\prime}}}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}})^{T})\right)

Combining these equations,

\displaystyle\left(M^{fact}_{\tau}(v{w^{T}})+M^{fact}_{{\tau}^{T}}(w{v^{T}})\right)-\left(M^{orth}_{\tau}(v{w^{T}})+M^{orth}_{{\tau}^{T}}(w{v^{T}})\right)\preceq

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\},\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}:\atop\gamma\text{ or }\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial }}\sum_{j>0}{\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}}{\prod_{i:\gamma_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma_{i}})|}}\prod_{i:\gamma^{\prime}_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}})|}}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{P_{1},\cdots,P_{j}:P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j}{N(P_{i})}\right)B_{norm}(\tau_{P_{j}})}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{N(\gamma)B(\gamma){c(\gamma)}}{N(\gamma^{\prime})B(\gamma^{\prime}){c(\gamma^{\prime})}}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(v^{-\gamma}(v^{-\gamma})^{T})+\frac{N(\gamma^{\prime})B(\gamma^{\prime}){c(\gamma^{\prime})}}{N(\gamma)B(\gamma){c(\gamma)}}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma^{\prime}}}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}})^{T})\right)

From the conditions on
B(\gamma)
and
N(\gamma)
,

1.

B_{norm}(\tau_{P_{j}})\leq B(\gamma)B(\gamma^{\prime})B_{norm}(\tau)

2.

\displaystyle\sum_{j>0}{\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}}{\left(\prod_{i:\gamma_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma_{i}})|}}\right)\left(\prod_{i:\gamma^{\prime}_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}})|}}\right)}}

\displaystyle\sum_{P_{1},\cdots,P_{j}:P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j}{N(P_{i})}\right)}\leq\frac{N(\gamma)N(\gamma^{\prime})}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}}

Putting these equations together,

\displaystyle\left(M^{fact}_{\tau}(v{w^{T}})+M^{fact}_{{\tau}^{T}}(w{v^{T}})\right)-\left(M^{orth}_{\tau}(v{w^{T}})+M^{orth}_{{\tau}^{T}}(w{v^{T}})\right)\preceq

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\},\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}:\atop\gamma\text{ or }\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial }}{\frac{B(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B_{norm}(\tau){c(\gamma)}}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}{c(\gamma^{\prime})}}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(v^{-\gamma}(v^{-\gamma})^{T})}+

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\},\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}:\atop\gamma\text{ or }\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial }}{\frac{B(\gamma^{\prime})^{2}N(\gamma^{\prime})^{2}B_{norm}(\tau){c(\gamma^{\prime})}}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}{c(\gamma)}}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma^{\prime}}}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}})^{T})}

Now observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\},\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}:\atop\gamma\text{ or }\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial }}{\frac{B(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B_{norm}(\tau){c(\gamma)}}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}{c(\gamma^{\prime})}}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(v^{-\gamma}(v^{-\gamma})^{T})}\preceq

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|{c(\gamma^{\prime})}}}\right)B_{norm}(\tau)M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\tau}}}(vv^{T})+

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}}{\left(\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}}{\frac{1}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}{c(\gamma^{\prime})}}}\right)\frac{B(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B_{norm}(\tau){c(\gamma)}}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(v^{-\gamma}(v^{-\gamma})^{T})}\preceq

\displaystyle{\varepsilon^{\prime}}B_{norm}(\tau)M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\tau}}}(vv^{T})+2\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}}{\frac{B(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B_{norm}(\tau){c(\gamma)}}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(v^{-\gamma}(v^{-\gamma})^{T})}

Following similar logic,

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\},\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}:\atop\gamma\text{ or }\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial }}{\frac{B(\gamma^{\prime})^{2}N(\gamma^{\prime})^{2}B_{norm}(\tau){c(\gamma^{\prime})}}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}{c(\gamma)}}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma^{\prime}}}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}})^{T})}\preceq

\displaystyle{\varepsilon^{\prime}}B_{norm}(\tau)M^{fact}_{Id_{V_{\tau}}}(ww^{T})+2\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}}{\frac{B(\gamma^{\prime})^{2}N(\gamma^{\prime})^{2}B_{norm}(\tau){c(\gamma^{\prime})}}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma^{\prime}}}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}})^{T})}

Putting everything together,

\displaystyle\left(M^{fact}_{\tau}(v{w^{T}})+M^{fact}_{{\tau}^{T}}(w{v^{T}})\right)-\left(M^{orth}_{\tau}(v{w^{T}})+M^{orth}_{{\tau}^{T}}(w{v^{T}})\right)\preceq

\displaystyle{\varepsilon^{\prime}}B_{norm}(\tau)M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\tau}}}(vv^{T})+2\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}}{\frac{B(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B_{norm}(\tau){c(\gamma)}}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(v^{-\gamma}(v^{-\gamma})^{T})}+

\displaystyle{\varepsilon^{\prime}}B_{norm}(\tau)M^{fact}_{Id_{V_{\tau}}}(ww^{T})+2\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}}{\frac{B(\gamma^{\prime})^{2}N(\gamma^{\prime})^{2}B_{norm}(\tau){c(\gamma^{\prime})}}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma^{\prime}}}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}}(w^{-\gamma^{\prime}})^{T})}

as needed.

Using Lemma
8.21
we have the following corollaries:

, if the norm bounds and the conditions on

8.5
Proof of the Main Theorem

We now prove the following theorem which is a slight modification of Theorem
8.1
and which implies Theorem
8.1
.

Theorem 8.24
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
and all
\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,\frac{1}{20}]
, for any moment matrix

\Lambda=\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{orth}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})}+\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{M^{orth}_{\tau}(H_{\tau})}},

if we have that for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime},||M_{\alpha}||\leq B_{norm}(\alpha)
and
B(\gamma)
,
N(\gamma)
, and
c(\alpha)
are functions such that

1.

For all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}
, and all intersection patterns
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,\gamma^{\prime}}
,

B_{norm}(\tau_{P})\leq B(\gamma)B(\gamma^{\prime})B_{norm}(\tau)

2.

For all composable
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
,
B(\gamma_{1})B(\gamma_{2})=B(\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2})
.

3.

\forall U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

4.

\forall V\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,V}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|c(\gamma)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

5.

\forall U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

6.

For all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\}
, and
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j>0}{\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}}{\prod_{i:\gamma_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma_{i}})|}}\prod_{i:\gamma^{\prime}_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}})|}}}}\sum_{P_{1},\cdots,P_{j}:P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j}{N(P_{i})}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{N(\gamma)N(\gamma^{\prime})}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}}

and we have SOS-symmetric coefficient matrices
\{H^{\prime}_{\gamma}:\gamma\in\Gamma\}
such that the following conditions hold:

1.

For all
U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}
,
H_{Id_{U}}\succeq 0

2.

For all
U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}
and
\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}
,

\left[{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}&B_{norm}(\tau)H_{\tau}\\
B_{norm}(\tau)H^{T}_{\tau}&\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}\end{array}}\right]\succeq 0

3.

For all
U,V\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}
where
w(U)>w(V)
and all
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,V}
,

c(\gamma)^{2}{N(\gamma)}^{2}{B(\gamma)^{2}}H^{-\gamma,\gamma}_{Id_{V}}\preceq H^{\prime}_{\gamma}

then

\Lambda\succeq\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}{(H_{Id_{U}})}}\right)-3\left(\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma},H_{Id_{U}})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}}}\right)Id_{sym}

If it is also true that

\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}{(H_{Id_{U}})}}\succeq 6\left(\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma},H_{Id_{U}})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}}}\right)Id_{sym}

then
\Lambda\succeq 0
.

Proof.

We make the following observations:

1.

By Theorem
8.2
,

\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})}+\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{M^{fact}_{\tau}(H_{\tau})}\succeq(1-2\varepsilon^{\prime})\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})}}

2.

By Corollary
8.22
,

\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\left(M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})-M^{orth}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})\right)}\preceq{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})}+2\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U}}{\frac{M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{c(\gamma)|Aut(U_{\gamma})|}}}

3.

By Corollary
8.23
,

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{\left(M^{fact}_{\tau}(H_{\tau})-M^{orth}_{\tau}(H_{\tau})\right)}}\preceq

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{\left(\frac{2{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})+4\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U}}{\frac{B(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}{c(\gamma)}}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|\cdot|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(H_{Id_{U}}^{-\gamma,\gamma})}\right)}}\preceq

\displaystyle 2{\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{2}\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})}+4{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U}}{\frac{M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{c(\gamma)|Aut(U_{\gamma})|}}}

4.

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U}}{\frac{M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{c(\gamma)|Aut(U_{\gamma})|}}}=\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U}}{\frac{M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(H_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}})+\left(M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma})-M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(H_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}})\right)}{c(\gamma)|Aut(U_{\gamma})|}}}\preceq

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U}}{\frac{M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(H_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}})}{c(\gamma)|Aut(U_{\gamma})|}}}+\left(\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{d_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma},H_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}})}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|c(\gamma)}}}\right)Id_{sym}\preceq

\displaystyle{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{U}(H_{Id_{U}})}+\left(\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{d_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma},H_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}})}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|c(\gamma)}}}\right)Id_{sym}

Putting everything together,

\displaystyle\Lambda=\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{orth}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})}+\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{M^{orth}_{\tau}(H_{\tau})}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})}+\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{M^{fact}_{\tau}(H_{\tau})}}+\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\left(M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})-M^{orth}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})\right)}+

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{\left(M^{fact}_{\tau}(H_{\tau})-M^{orth}_{\tau}(H_{\tau})\right)}}\succeq

\displaystyle(1-3{\varepsilon^{\prime}}-2{\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{2})\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})}-(2+4\varepsilon^{\prime})\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U}}{\frac{M^{fact}_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{c(\gamma)|Aut(U_{\gamma})|}}}\succeq

\displaystyle(1-5{\varepsilon^{\prime}}-6{\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{2})\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})}-(2+4\varepsilon^{\prime})\left(\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{d_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma},H_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}})}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|c(\gamma)}}}\right)Id_{sym}\succeq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}})}-3\left(\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{d_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma},H_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}})}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|c(\gamma)}}}\right)Id_{sym}

9
Choosing the functions
B_{norm}(\alpha)
,
B(\gamma)
,
N(\gamma)
, and
c(\alpha)

In this subsection, we give functions
B_{norm}(\alpha)
,
B(\gamma)
,
N(\gamma)
, and

which satisfy the conditions needed for our machinery.

9.1
Theorem Statements

Recall the following definitions from Section
7.10
.

Definition 9.1
.

We define
S_{\alpha}
to be the leftmost minimum vertex separator of
\alpha

Definition 9.2

(Simplified Isolated Vertices)
.

Under our simplifying assumptions, we define

I_{\alpha}=\{v\in W_{\alpha}:v\text{ is not incident to any edges in }E(\alpha)\}

Theorem 9.3

(Simplified
B_{norm}(\alpha)
,
B(\gamma)
,
N(\gamma)
, and
c(\alpha)
)
.

Under our simplifying assumptions, for all
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
and all
D_{V}\in\mathbb{N}
, if we take

1.

q=3\left\lceil{{D_{V}}ln(n)+\frac{ln(\frac{1}{\varepsilon})}{3}+{D_{V}}ln(5)+3{D^{2}_{V}}ln(2)}\right\rceil

2.

B_{vertex}=6{D_{V}}\sqrt[4]{2eq}

3.

B_{norm}(\alpha)={B_{vertex}^{|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|+|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|}}n^{\frac{w(V(\alpha))+w(I_{\alpha})-w(S_{\alpha})}{2}}

4.

B(\gamma)=B_{vertex}^{|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|}n^{\frac{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}{2}}

5.

N(\gamma)=(3D_{V})^{2|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|}

6.

c(\alpha)=\frac{5(3D_{V})^{|U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}|+|V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}|+2|E(\alpha)|}2^{|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha})|}}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}

then the following conditions hold:

1.

With probability at least
(1-\varepsilon)
,
\forall\alpha\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
||M_{\alpha}||\leq B_{norm}(\alpha)

2.

For all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\}
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}
, and intersection patterns
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,\gamma^{\prime}}
,

B_{norm}(\tau_{P})\leq B(\gamma)B(\gamma^{\prime})B_{norm}(\tau)

3.

For all composable
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
,
B(\gamma_{1})B(\gamma_{2})=B(\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2})
.

4.

\forall U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

5.

\forall V\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,V}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|c(\gamma)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

6.

\forall U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

7.

For all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\}
, and
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j>0}{\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}}{\prod_{i:\gamma_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma_{i}})|}}\prod_{i:\gamma^{\prime}_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}})|}}}}\sum_{P_{1},\cdots,P_{j}:P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j}{N(P_{i})}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{N(\gamma)N(\gamma^{\prime})}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}}

9.1.1
General functions
B_{norm}(\alpha)
,
B(\gamma)
,
N(\gamma)
, and
c(\alpha)
*

Recall the following definitions from Section
7.10.1
.

Definition 9.4

(
S_{\alpha,min}
and
S_{\alpha,max}
)
.

Given a shape
\alpha\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, define
S_{\alpha,min}
to be the leftmost minimum vertex separator of
\alpha
if all edges with multiplicity at least
2
are deleted and define
S_{\alpha,max}
to be the leftmost minimum vertex separator of
\alpha
if all edges with multiplicity at least
2
are present.

Definition 9.5

(General
I_{\alpha}
)
.

Given a shape
\alpha
, define
I_{\alpha}
to be the set of vertices in
V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha})
such that all edges incident with that vertex have multplicity at least
2
.

Definition 9.6

(
B_{\Omega}
)
.

We take
B_{\Omega}(j)
to be a non-decreasing function such that for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
E_{\Omega}[x^{j}]\leq B_{\Omega}(j)^{j}

Definition 9.7
.

For all
i
, we define
h^{+}_{i}
to be the polynomial
h_{i}
where we make all of the coefficients have positive sign.

Lemma 9.8
.

If
\Omega=N(0,1)
then we can take
B_{\Omega}(j)=\sqrt{j}
and we have that

Theorem 9.9

(General
B_{norm}(\alpha)
,
B(\gamma)
,
N(\gamma)
, and
c(\alpha)
)
.

For all
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
and all
D_{V},D_{E}\in\mathbb{N}
, if we take

1.

q=\left\lceil{3{D_{V}}ln(n)+ln(\frac{1}{\varepsilon})+{(3D_{V})^{k}}ln(D_{E}+1)+3{D_{V}}ln(5)}\right\rceil

2.

B_{vertex}=6q{D_{V}}

3.

B_{edge}(e)=2h^{+}_{l_{e}}(B_{\Omega}(6{D_{V}}D_{E}))\max_{j\in[0,3{D_{V}}D_{E}]}{\left\{\left(h^{+}_{j}(B_{\Omega}(2qj))\right)^{\frac{l_{e}}{\max{\{j,l_{e}\}}}}\right\}}

4.

B_{norm}(\alpha)=2e{B_{vertex}^{|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|+|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|}}\left(\prod_{e\in E(\alpha)}{B_{edge}(e)}\right)n^{\frac{w(V(\alpha))+w(I_{\alpha})-w(S_{\alpha})}{2}}

5.

B(\gamma)=B_{vertex}^{|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|}\left(\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}{B_{edge}(e)}\right)n^{\frac{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}{2}}

6.

N(\gamma)=(3D_{V})^{2|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|}

7.

c(\alpha)=\frac{5(3{t_{max}}D_{V})^{|U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}|+|V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}|+k|E(\alpha)|}(2t_{max})^{|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha})|}}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}

then the following conditions hold:

1.

With probability at least
(1-\varepsilon)
,
\forall\alpha\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
||M_{\alpha}||\leq B_{norm}(\alpha)

2.

For all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\}
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}
, and intersection patterns
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,\gamma^{\prime}}
,

B_{norm}(\tau_{P})\leq B(\gamma)B(\gamma^{\prime})B_{norm}(\tau)

3.

For all composable
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
,
B(\gamma_{1})B(\gamma_{2})=B(\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2})
.

4.

\forall U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

5.

\forall V\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,V}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|c(\gamma)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

6.

\forall U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

7.

For all
\tau\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,U_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{U_{\tau}}\}
, and
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{*,V_{\tau}}\cup\{Id_{V_{\tau}}\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j>0}{\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}}{\prod_{i:\gamma_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma_{i}})|}}\prod_{i:\gamma^{\prime}_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}})|}}}}\sum_{P_{1},\cdots,P_{j}:P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j}{N(P_{i})}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{N(\gamma)N(\gamma^{\prime})}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}}

Remark 9.10
.

Recall that if
\Omega=N(0,1)
then we may take
B_{\Omega}(j)=\sqrt{j}
and we have that

h^{+}_{j}(x)\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{j!}}(x^{2}+j)^{\frac{j}{2}}\leq\left(\frac{e}{j}(x^{2}+j)\right)^{\frac{j}{2}}

Thus, when
\Omega=N(0,1)
we can take

B_{edge}(e)=2\left(\frac{e}{l_{e}}(6{D_{V}}D_{E}+l_{e})\right)^{l_{e}}\left(e(6{D_{V}}{D_{E}}q+1)\right)^{l_{e}}\leq\left(400{D^{2}_{V}}{D^{2}_{E}}q\right)^{l_{e}}

9.2
Choosing
B_{norm}(\alpha)

We need matrix norm bounds which hold for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
. For convenience, we recall the definition of
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
below.

Definition 9.11

(
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
)
.

We define
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
to be the set of all shapes
\alpha
such that

1.

|V(\alpha)|\leq 3D_{V}

2.*

\forall e\in E(\alpha),l_{e}\leq D_{E}

3.*

All edges
e\in E(\alpha)
have multiplicity at most
3D_{V}
.

To obtain such norm bounds, we start with the norm bounds in the graph matrix norm bound paper. We then modify these bounds as follows:

1.

We make the bounds more compatible with the conditions of our machinery. To do this, we upper bound many of the terms in the norm bound by
B_{vertex}^{|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|+|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|}
where
B_{vertex}
is a function of our parameters. In general, we will also need to upper bound some of the terms by
\prod_{e\in E(\alpha)}(B_{edge}(e))
where
B_{edge}(e)
is a function of
l_{e}
,
\Omega
, and our parameters.

2.

We generalize the bounds so that they apply to improper shapes as well as proper shapes. Under our simplifying assumptions, all we need to do here is to take isolated vertices into account. In general, we also need to handle multi-edges.

9.2.1
Simplified
B_{norm}(\alpha)

Under our simplifying assumptions, we start with the following norm bound from the updated graph matrix norm bound paper
[
AMP20
]
:

Theorem 9.12

(Simplified Graph Matrix Norm Bounds)
.

Under our simplifying assumptions, for all
\varepsilon>0
and all proper shapes
\alpha
, taking
c_{\alpha}=|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha})|+|S_{\alpha}\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})|
,

Pr\left(||M_{\alpha}||>(2|V_{\alpha}\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})|)^{|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})|}(2eq)^{\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2}}n^{\frac{w(V(\alpha))-w(S_{\alpha})}{2}}\right)<\varepsilon

where
q=3\left\lceil\frac{ln(\frac{n^{w(S_{\alpha})}}{\varepsilon})}{3c_{\alpha}}\right\rceil

Corollary 9.13
.

For all shapes
\alpha
and all
\varepsilon>0
,

Pr\left(||M_{\alpha}||>\left(2|V_{\alpha}|\sqrt[4]{2eq}\right)^{|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|+|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|}n^{\frac{w(V(\alpha))+w(I_{\alpha})-w(S_{\alpha})}{2}}\right)<\varepsilon

where
q=3\left\lceil\frac{ln(\frac{n^{w(S_{\alpha})}}{\varepsilon})}{3c_{\alpha}}\right\rceil
.

Proof.

Observe that adding an isolated vertex to
\alpha
is equivalent to multiplying
M_{\alpha}
by
n-|V(\alpha)|
. Thus, if the bound holds for all proper
\alpha
then it will hold for improper
\alpha
as well.

We now make the following observations:

1.

|S_{\alpha}\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})|\leq|U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}|
, so
c_{\alpha}=|W_{\alpha}|+|S_{\alpha}\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})|\leq|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|
. Similarly,
|S_{\alpha}\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})|\leq|V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}|
, so
c_{\alpha}\leq|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|
. Thus,
c_{\alpha}\leq\frac{|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|+|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|}{2}
.

2.

|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})|\leq|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|+|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|

Thus, by Theorem
9.12
, for all proper shapes
\alpha
and all
\varepsilon>0
,

Pr\left(||M_{\alpha}||>\left(2|V_{\alpha}|\sqrt[4]{2eq}\right)^{|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|+|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|}n^{\frac{w(V(\alpha))+w(I_{\alpha})-w(S_{\alpha})}{2}}\right)<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}

where
q=3\left\lceil\frac{ln(\frac{n^{w(S_{\alpha})}}{\varepsilon})}{3c_{\alpha}}\right\rceil
.

Corollary 9.14
.

For all
z\in\mathbb{N}
and all
\varepsilon>0
, taking
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon}{5^{z}2^{z^{2}}}
, with probability at least
1-\varepsilon
we have that for all shapes
\alpha
such that
|V(\alpha)|\leq z
,

||M_{\alpha}||\leq\left(2|V_{\alpha}|\sqrt[4]{2eq}\right)^{|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|+|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|}n^{\frac{w(V(\alpha))+w(I_{\alpha})-w(S_{\alpha})}{2}}

where
q=3\left\lceil\frac{ln(\frac{n^{w(S_{\alpha})}}{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}})}{3c_{\alpha}}\right\rceil
.

Proof.

This result can be proved from Corollary
9.13
using a union bound and the following proposition:

Proposition 9.15
.

Under our simplifying assumptions, for all
z\in\mathbb{N}
, there are at most
5^{z}2^{z^{2}}
proper shapes
\alpha
such that
V(\alpha)\leq z
.

Proof.

Observe that we can construct any proper shape
\alpha
with at most
m
vertices as follows:

1.

Start with
z
vertices
v_{1},\ldots,v_{z}
.

2.

For each vertex
v_{i}
, choose whether
v_{i}\in V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}
,
v_{i}\in U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}
,
v_{i}\in V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}
,
v_{i}\in U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha}
, or
v_{i}\notin V(\alpha)
.

3.

For each pair of vertices
v_{i},v_{j}\in V(\alpha)
, choose whether or not
(v_{i},v_{j})\in E(\alpha)

Corollary 9.16
.

For all
D_{V}\in\mathbb{N}
and all
\varepsilon>0
, taking

q=3\left\lceil\frac{ln(\frac{5^{3D_{V}}2^{9D^{2}_{V}}n^{3D_{V}}}{\varepsilon})}{3}\right\rceil=3\left\lceil{{D_{V}}ln(n)+\frac{ln(\frac{1}{\varepsilon})}{3}+{D_{V}}ln(5)+3{D^{2}_{V}}ln(2)}\right\rceil,

B_{vertex}=6{D_{V}}\sqrt[4]{2eq}
, and

B_{norm}(\alpha)={B_{vertex}^{|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|+|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|}}n^{\frac{w(V(\alpha))+w(I_{\alpha})-w(S_{\alpha})}{2}},

with probability at least
(1-\varepsilon)
we have that for all shapes
\alpha\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
||M_{\alpha}||\leq B_{norm}(\alpha)

Proof.

This follows from Corollary
9.14
and the fact that for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
w(S_{\alpha})\leq|V(\alpha)|\leq 3D_{V}

9.2.2
General
B_{norm}(\alpha)

In general, we start with the following norm bound from the updated graph matrix norm bound paper
[
AMP20
]
:

Theorem 9.17

(General Graph Matrix Norm Bounds)
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
and all proper shapes
\alpha
, taking
q=\lceil{ln(\frac{n^{w(S_{\alpha})}}{\varepsilon})}\rceil

P\left(||M_{\alpha}||>2e(2q|V(\alpha)|)^{|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})|}\left(\prod_{e\in E(\alpha)}{h^{+}_{l_{e}}(B_{\Omega}(2q{l_{e}}))}\right)n^{\frac{(w(V(\alpha))-w(S_{\alpha}))}{2}}\right)<\varepsilon

Corollary 9.18
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, for all
z,l_{max},m\in\mathbb{N}
, taking
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon}{5^{z}(l_{max}+1)^{z^{k}}}
, with probability at least
1-\varepsilon
, for all shapes
\alpha
such that

1.

|V(\alpha)|\leq z
.

2.

All edges in
E(\alpha)
have label at most
l_{max}
.

3.

All edges in
E(\alpha)
have multiplicity at most
m
.

,

\displaystyle||M_{\alpha}||\leq

\displaystyle 2e(2q|V(\alpha)|)^{|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|+|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|}\left(\prod_{e\in E(\alpha)}{2h^{+}_{l_{e}}(B_{\Omega}(2ml_{max}))\max_{j\in[0,ml_{max}]}{\left\{\left(h^{+}_{j}(B_{\Omega}(2qj))\right)^{\frac{l_{e}}{\max{\{j,l_{e}\}}}}\right\}}}\right)

\displaystyle n^{\frac{w(V(\alpha))+w(I_{\alpha})-w(S_{\alpha,min})}{2}}

where
q=\left\lceil{ln\left(\frac{n^{w(S_{\alpha,max})}}{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}\right)}\right\rceil

Proof.

Observe that for each
\alpha
which has multi-edges, we can write
M_{\alpha}=\sum_{i}{{c_{i}}M_{\alpha_{i}}}
where each
\alpha_{i}
has no multiple edges. We first upper bound
\sum_{i}{|c_{i}|}
.

Lemma 9.19
.

For any
a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
, taking
p_{max}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}{a_{i}}
and writing
\prod_{i=1}^{m}{h_{a_{i}}}=\sum_{k=0}^{p_{max}}{{c_{k}}h_{k}}
,

\sum_{k=0}^{p_{max}}{|c_{k}|}\leq(p_{max}+1)\prod_{i=1}^{m}{h^{+}_{a_{i}}(B_{\Omega}(2p_{max}))}\leq\prod_{i=1}^{m}{2h^{+}_{a_{i}}(B_{\Omega}(2p_{max}))}

Proof.

Suppose
\prod_{i=1}^{m}(h_{a_{i}}(x))^{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{2p_{max}}u_{k}x^{k}
and
\prod_{i=1}^{m}(h^{+}_{a_{i}}(x))^{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{p_{max}}v_{k}x^{k}
. Then, note that
|u_{k}|\leq v_{k}
and so,

E_{\Omega}[\prod_{i=1}^{m}(h_{a_{i}}(x))^{2}]=\sum_{k=0}^{2p_{max}}u_{k}E_{\Omega}[x^{k}]\leq\sum_{k=0}^{2p_{max}}v_{k}|E_{\Omega}[x^{k}]|\leq\sum_{k=0}^{2p_{max}}v_{k}(B_{\Omega}(2p_{max}))^{k}=\prod_{i=1}^{m}(h_{a_{i}}^{+}(B_{\Omega}(2p_{max}))^{2}

Therefore, using the fact that
h_{k}
form an orthonormal basis,

\sum_{k=0}^{p_{max}}c_{k}^{2}=E_{\Omega}[(\sum_{k=0}^{p_{max}}c_{k}h_{k}(x))^{2}]=E_{\Omega}[\prod_{i=1}^{m}(h_{a_{i}}(x))^{2}]\leq\prod_{i=1}^{m}(h_{a_{i}}^{+}(B_{\Omega}(2p_{max}))^{2}

This implies

(\sum_{k=0}^{p_{max}}|c_{k}|)^{2}\leq(p_{max}+1)(\sum_{k=0}^{p_{max}}c_{k}^{2})\leq(p_{max}+1)\prod_{i=1}^{m}(h_{a_{i}}^{+}(B_{\Omega}(2p_{max}))^{2}

Taking square roots gives the inequality.

Corollary 9.20
.

For any shape
\alpha
such that every edge of
\alpha
has multiplicity at most
m
and label at most
l_{max}
, if we write

M_{\alpha}=\sum_{i}{{c_{i}}M_{\alpha_{i}}}
where each
\alpha_{i}
has no multi-edges then
\sum_{i}{|c_{i}|}\leq\prod_{e\in E(\alpha)}{2h^{+}_{l_{e}}(B_{\Omega}(2ml_{max}))}

The result now follows from Theorem
9.17
and the following observations:

1.

|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})|\leq|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|+|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|
.

2.

For any
\alpha
, writing
M_{\alpha}=\sum_{i}{{c_{i}}M_{\alpha_{i}}}
where each
\alpha_{i}
has no multi-edges, for all
\alpha_{i}
,

w(V(\alpha_{i}))+w(I_{\alpha_{i}})-w(S_{\alpha_{i}})\leq w(V(\alpha))+w(I_{\alpha})-w(S_{\alpha,min})

3.

For any
a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
such that
\forall i^{\prime}\in[m],a_{i^{\prime}}\leq l_{max}
, for all
j\in[0,ml_{max}]

h^{+}_{j}(B_{\Omega}(2qj))\leq\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{m}{\left(h^{+}_{j}(B_{\Omega}(2qj))\right)^{\frac{a_{i^{\prime}}}{\max{\{j,a_{i^{\prime}}\}}}}}\leq\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{m}{\max_{j^{\prime}\in[0,ml_{max}]}{\left\{\left(h^{+}_{j^{\prime}}(B_{\Omega}(2qj^{\prime}))\right)^{\frac{a_{i^{\prime}}}{\max{\{j^{\prime},a_{i^{\prime}}\}}}}\right\}}}

Proposition 9.21
.

For all
z,l_{max}\in\mathbb{N}
, there are at most
5^{z}(l_{max}+1)^{z^{k}}
proper shapes
\alpha
such that
|V(\alpha)|\leq z
and every edge in
E(\alpha)
.

Proof.

This can be proved in the same way as before. Observe that we can construct any proper shape
\alpha
with at most
z
vertices as follows:

1.

Start with
z
vertices
v_{1},\ldots,v_{z}
.

2.

For each vertex
v_{i}
, choose whether
v_{i}\in V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}
,
v_{i}\in U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}
,
v_{i}\in V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}
,
v_{i}\in U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha}
, or
v_{i}\notin V(\alpha)
.

3.

For each
k
tuple of vertices in
V(\alpha)
, choose the label of the hyperedge between these vertices (or
0
if the hyperedge is not in
E(\alpha)
).

Corollary 9.22
.

For all
D_{V},D_{E}\in\mathbb{N}
and all
\varepsilon>0
, taking

B_{norm}(\alpha)=2e{B_{vertex}^{|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|+|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|}}\left(\prod_{e\in E(\alpha)}{B_{edge}(e)}\right)n^{\frac{w(V(\alpha))+w(I_{\alpha})-w(S_{\alpha})}{2}}

where

1.

q=\left\lceil{ln\left(\frac{n^{3D_{V}}}{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}\right)}\right\rceil=\left\lceil{3{D_{V}}ln(n)+ln(\frac{1}{\varepsilon})+{(3D_{V})^{k}}ln(D_{E}+1)+3{D_{V}}ln(5)}\right\rceil

2.

B_{vertex}=6q{D_{V}}

3.

B_{edge}(e)=2h^{+}_{l_{e}}(B_{\Omega}(6{D_{V}}D_{E}))\max_{j\in[0,3{D_{V}}D_{E}]}{\left\{\left(h^{+}_{j}(B_{\Omega}(2qj))\right)^{\frac{l_{e}}{\max{\{j,l_{e}\}}}}\right\}}

with probability at least
(1-\varepsilon)
, for all shapes
\alpha\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
||M_{\alpha}||\leq B_{norm}(\alpha)
.

9.3
Choosing
B(\gamma)

We now describe how to choose the function
B(\gamma)
. Recall that we want the following conditions to hold:

1.

For all
\gamma,\tau,\gamma^{\prime}
and all intersection patterns
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,\gamma^{\prime}}
,

B_{norm}(\tau_{P})\leq B(\gamma)B(\gamma^{\prime})B_{norm}(\tau)

2.

For all composable
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
,
B(\gamma_{1})B(\gamma_{2})=B(\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2})
.

The most important part of choosing
B(\gamma)
is to make sure that the factors of
n
are controlled. For this, we use the following intersection tradeoff lemma. Under our simplifying assumptions, this lemma follows from
[
BHK+16
, Lemma 7.12]
. We defer the general proof of this lemma to the end of this section.

Lemma 9.23

(Intersection Tradeoff Lemma)
.

For all
\gamma,\tau,\gamma^{\prime}
and all intersection patterns
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,\gamma^{\prime}}
,

w(V(\tau_{P}))+w(I_{\tau_{P}})-w(S_{\tau_{P},min})\leq w(V(\tau))+w(I_{\tau})-w(S_{\tau,min})+w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})+w(V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus U_{\gamma^{\prime}})

Based on this intersection tradeoff lemma, we can choose the function
B(\gamma)
as follows.

Corollary 9.24
.

If we take

B_{norm}(\alpha)=C\cdot B_{vertex}^{|V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}|+|V(\alpha)\setminus V_{\alpha}|}\left(\prod_{e\in E(\alpha)}{B_{edge}(e)}\right)n^{\frac{w(V(\alpha))+w(I_{\alpha})-w(S_{\alpha})}{2}}

for some constant
C>0
and take

B(\gamma)=B_{vertex}^{|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|}\left(\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}{B_{edge}(e)}\right)n^{\frac{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}{2}}

then the following conditions hold:

1.

For all
\gamma,\tau,\gamma^{\prime}
and all intersection patterns
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,\gamma^{\prime}}
,

B_{norm}(\tau_{P})\leq B(\gamma)B(\gamma^{\prime})B_{norm}(\tau)

2.

For all composable
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
,
B(\gamma_{1})B(\gamma_{2})=B(\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2})
.

Proof.

We have that

B_{norm}(\tau_{P})=B_{vertex}^{|V(\tau_{P})\setminus U_{\tau_{P}}|+|V(\tau_{P})\setminus V_{\tau_{P}}|}\left(\prod_{e\in E(\tau_{P})}{B_{edge}(e)}\right)n^{\frac{w(V(\tau_{P}))+w(I_{\tau_{P}})-w(S_{\tau_{P}})}{2}}

and

\displaystyle B(\gamma)B(\gamma^{\prime})B_{norm}(\tau)

\displaystyle=B_{vertex}^{|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus U_{\gamma^{\prime}}|+|V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus V_{\gamma^{\prime}}|+|V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}|+|V(\tau)\setminus V_{\tau}|}

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)\cup E(\gamma^{\prime})\cup E(\tau)}{B_{edge}(e)}\right)n^{\frac{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})+w(V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus U_{\gamma^{\prime}})+w(V(\tau))+w(I_{\tau})-w(S_{\tau})}{2}}

The first condition now follows immediately from the following observations:

1.

\displaystyle|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus U_{\gamma^{\prime}}|+|V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus V_{\gamma^{\prime}}|+|V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}|+|V(\tau)\setminus V_{\tau}|

\displaystyle=|V(\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})\setminus U_{\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}|+|V(\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})\setminus V_{\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}|\geq|V(\tau_{P})\setminus U_{\tau_{P}}|+|V(\tau_{P})\setminus V_{\tau_{P}}|

2.

E(\tau_{P})=E(\gamma)\cup E(\tau)\cup E({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})
so

\prod_{e\in E(\tau_{P})}{B_{edge}(e)}=\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)\cup E(\gamma^{\prime})\cup E(\tau)}{B_{edge}(e)}
.

3.

By the intersection tradeoff lemma,

w(V(\tau_{P}))+w(I_{\tau_{P}})-w(S_{\tau_{P}})\leq w(V(\tau))+w(I_{\tau})-w(S_{\tau})+w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})+w(V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus U_{\gamma^{\prime}})

The second condition follows from the form of
B(\gamma)
.

9.4
Choosing
N(\gamma)

To choose
N(\gamma)
, we use the following lemma:

Lemma 9.25
.

For all
D_{V}\in\mathbb{N}
, for all composable
\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
such that
|V(\gamma)|\leq D_{V}
,
|V(\tau)|\leq D_{V}
, and
|V(\gamma^{\prime})|\leq D_{V}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j>0}{\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}}{\prod_{i:\gamma_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma_{i}})|}}\prod_{i:\gamma^{\prime}_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}})|}}}}\sum_{P_{1},\cdots,P_{j}:P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j}{N(P_{i})}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{(3D_{V})}^{2(|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus V_{\gamma^{\prime}}|)+(|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus U_{\gamma^{\prime}}|)}}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}}

Proof sketch.

Observe that aside from the orderings (which are canceled out by the
|Aut(U_{\gamma_{i}})|
and
|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}})|
factors), the intersection patterns
\{P_{i}:i\in[j]\}
are determined by the following data on each vertex
v\in(V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma})\cup(V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})\setminus V_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}})
:

1.

The first
i\in[j]
such that
v\in(V(\gamma_{i})\setminus V_{\gamma_{i}})\cup(V({\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T})\setminus V_{{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}})
. There are at most
j
possibilities for this.

2.

A vertex
u
(if one exists) in
V(\gamma_{i-1}\circ\ldots\circ\gamma_{1}\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}_{1}}^{T}\ldots\circ{\gamma^{\prime}_{i-1}}^{T})
such that
u
and
v
are equal. There are at most
3D_{V}
possibilities for this.

Using these observations and taking
j_{max}=|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus V_{\gamma^{\prime}}|
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j>0}{\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}}{\prod_{i:\gamma_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma_{i}})|}}\prod_{i:\gamma^{\prime}_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}})|}}}}\sum_{P_{1},\cdots,P_{j}:P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}}{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{j_{max}}{\frac{{(3jD_{V})}^{|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus V_{\gamma^{\prime}}|}}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}}}

\displaystyle\leq j_{max}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)^{j_{max}}\frac{{(3D_{V})}^{2(|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus V_{\gamma^{\prime}}|)}}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}}

\displaystyle<\frac{{(3D_{V})}^{2(|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus V_{\gamma^{\prime}}|)}}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}}

Now recall that by Lemma ,
for any
\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}
and any intersection pattern
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}
,

N(P_{i})\leq|V(\tau_{P_{i}})|^{|V(\gamma_{i})\setminus U_{\gamma_{i}}|+|V(\gamma^{\prime}_{i})\setminus U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}|}

Thus, for any
P_{1},\cdots,P_{j}:P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}
,
\prod_{i=1}^{j}{N(P_{i})}\leq(3D_{V})^{|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus U_{\gamma^{\prime}}|}
.
Putting everything together, the result follows.

Corollary 9.26
.

For all
D_{V}\in\mathbb{N}
, if we take
N(\gamma)=(3D_{V})^{2|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|+|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|}
then for all composable
\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
such that
|V(\gamma)|\leq D_{V}
,
|V(\tau)|\leq D_{V}
, and
|V(\gamma^{\prime})|\leq D_{V}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j>0}{\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},j}}{\prod_{i:\gamma_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma_{i}})|}}\prod_{i:\gamma^{\prime}_{i}\text{ is non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}})|}}}}\sum_{P_{1},\cdots,P_{j}:P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma_{i},\tau_{P_{i-1}},{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}}^{T}}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j}{N(P_{i})}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{N(\gamma)N(\gamma^{\prime})}{(|Aut(U_{\gamma})|)^{1_{\gamma\text{ is non-trivial}}}(|Aut(U_{\gamma^{\prime}})|)^{1_{\gamma^{\prime}\text{ is non-trivial}}}}

9.5
Choosing
c(\alpha)

In this section, we describe how to choose
c(\alpha)
. For simplicity, we first describe how to choose
c(\alpha)
under our simplifying assumptions. We then describe the minor adjustments that are needed when we have hyperedges and multiple types of vertices.

Lemma 9.27
.

Under our simplifying assumptons, for all
U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}
,

\sum_{\alpha:U_{\alpha}\equiv U,\alpha\text{ is proper and non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})|(3D_{V})^{|U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}|+|V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}|+2|E(\alpha)|}2^{|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha})|}}}<5

Proof.

In order to choose
\alpha
, it is sufficient to choose the following:

1.

The number
j_{1}
of vertices in
U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}
, the number
j_{2}
of vertices in
V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}
, and the number
j_{3}
of vertices in
V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha})
.

2.

A mapping in
Aut(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})
determining how the vertices in
U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha}
match up with each other.

3.

The position of each vertex
u\in U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}
within
U_{\alpha}
(there are at most
|U_{\alpha}|\leq D_{V}
choices for this).

4.

The position of each vertex
v\in V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}
within
V_{\alpha}
(there are at most
|U_{\alpha}|\leq D_{V}
choices for this).

5.

The number
j_{4}
of edges in
E(\alpha)
.

6.

The endpoints of each edge in
E(\alpha)
.

This implies that for all
j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}\geq 0

\sum_{\alpha:U_{\alpha}\equiv U,|U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}|=j_{1},|V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}|=j_{2}\atop|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha})|=j_{3},|E(\alpha)|=j_{4}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})|(D_{V})^{j_{1}+j_{2}}(D_{V})^{2j_{4}}}}\leq 1

Using this, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha:U_{\alpha}\equiv U,\alpha\text{ is proper and non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})|(3D_{V})^{|U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}|+|V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}|+2|E(\alpha)|}2^{|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha})|}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}:j_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}+j_{4}\geq 1}{\frac{1}{3^{j_{1}+j_{2}}9^{j_{4}}2^{j_{3}}}}\leq 2\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)^{2}\frac{9}{8}-1<5

Corollary 9.28
.

For all
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
, if we take

c(\alpha)=\frac{5(3D_{V})^{|U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}|+|V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}|+2|E(\alpha)|}2^{|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha})|}}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}

then

1.

\forall U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

2.

\forall V\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,V}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|c(\gamma)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

3.

\forall U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

9.5.1
Choosing
c(\alpha)
in general*

When we have multiple types of vertices and hyperedges of arity
k
, Lemma
9.27
can be generalized as follows:

Lemma 9.29
.

Under our simplifying assumptons, for all
U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}
,

\sum_{\alpha:U_{\alpha}\equiv U,\alpha\text{ is proper and non-trivial}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})|(3D_{V}{t_{max}})^{|U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}|+|V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}|+k|E(\alpha)|}(2t_{max})^{|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha})|}}}<5

Proof sketch.

This can be proved in the same way as Lemma
9.27
with the following modifications:

1.

In addition to choosing the number of vertices in
U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}
,
V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}
, and
V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})
, we also have to choose the types of these vertices.

2.

For each hyperedge, we have to choose
k
endpoints rather than
2
endpoints.

Corollary 9.30
.

For all
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
, if we take

c(\alpha)=\frac{5(3{t_{max}}D_{V})^{|U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}|+|V_{\alpha}\setminus U_{\alpha}|+k|E(\alpha)|}(2t_{max})^{|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha})|}}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}

then

1.

\forall U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

2.

\forall V\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{*,V}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U_{\gamma})|c(\gamma)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

3.

\forall U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid},\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}}<\varepsilon^{\prime}

For technical reasons, we will need a more refined bound when the sum is over all shapes
\gamma
of at least a prescribed size.

Lemma 9.31
.

For all
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
, for the same choice of
c(\alpha)
as in
Corollary

9.30
, for any
U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
and integer
m\geq 1
, we have

\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}:|V(\gamma)|\geq|U|+m}\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{5\cdot 2^{m-1}}

Proof sketch.

The proof is similar to the proof of
Corollary

9.30
, but we now have the extra condition
j_{2}+j_{3}\geq m
in the proof of
Lemma

9.27
. Then,

\sum_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}:j_{2}+j_{3}\geq m}\frac{1}{3^{j_{1}+j_{2}}9^{j_{4}}2^{j_{3}}}\leq\sum_{j_{1},j_{4}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}}\frac{1}{2^{m}3^{j_{1}}9^{j_{4}}}=\frac{27}{16\cdot 2^{m}}\leq\frac{1}{2^{m-1}}

9.6
Proof of the Generalized Intersection Tradeoff Lemma

We now prove the generalized intersection tradeoff lemma, which in particular generalizes
[
BHK+16
, Lemma 7.12]
.

Lemma 9.32
.

For all
\gamma,\tau,\gamma^{\prime}
and all intersection patterns
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,\gamma^{\prime}}
,

w(V(\tau_{P}))+w(I_{\tau_{P}})-w(S_{\tau_{P},min})\leq w(V(\tau))+w(I_{\tau})-w(S_{\tau,min})+w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})+w(V(\gamma^{\prime})\setminus U_{\gamma^{\prime}})

Proof.

Definition 9.33
.

1.

We define
I_{LM}
to be the set of vertices which, after intersections, touch
\gamma
and
\tau
but not
{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
. In particular,
I_{LM}
consists of the vertices which result from intersecting a pair of vertices in
V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}
and
V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
and the vertices which are in
U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
and are not intersected with any other vertex.

2.

We define
I_{MR}
to be the set of vertices which, after intersections, touch
\tau
and
{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
but not
\gamma
. In particular,
I_{MR}
consists of the vertices which result from intersecting a pair of vertices in
V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
and
V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})\setminus U_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
and the vertices which are in
V_{\tau}\setminus U_{\tau}
and are not intersected with any other vertex.

3.

We define
I_{LR}
to be the set of vertices which, after intersections, touch
\gamma
and
{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
but not
\tau
. In particular,
I_{LR}
consists of the vertices which result from intersecting a pair of vertices in
V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}
and
V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})\setminus U_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
.

4.

We define
I_{LMR}
to be the set of vertices which, after intersections, touch
\gamma
,
\tau
, and
{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
. In particular,
I_{LMR}
consists of the vertices which result from intersecting a triple of vertices in
V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}
,
V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
, and
V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})\setminus U_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
, intersecting a pair of vertices in
V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}
and
V_{\tau}\setminus U_{\tau}
, intersecting a pair of vertices in
U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
and
V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})\setminus U_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
, and single vertices in
U_{\tau}\cap V_{\tau}
.

The main idea is as follows. A priori, any of the vertices in
I_{LM}\cup I_{MR}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR}
could become isolated. We handle this by keeping track of the following types of flows:

1.

Flows from
U_{\gamma}
to
I_{LM}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR}

2.

Flows from
I_{LR}\cup I_{MR}\cup I_{LMR}
to
V_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}

3.

Flows from
I_{LM}
to
I_{MR}
. For technical reasons, we also view vertices in
I_{LMR}
as having flow to themselves.

We then observe that flows to and from these vertices prevent these vertices from being isolated and can provide flow from
U_{\gamma}
to
V_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
, which gives a lower bound on
w(S_{\tau_{P}})
.

We now implement this idea.

Definition 9.34

(Flow Graph)
.

Given a shape
\alpha
, we define the directed graph
H_{\alpha}
as follows:

1.

For each vertex
v\in V(\alpha)
, we create two vertices
v_{in}
and
v_{out}
. We then create a directed edge from
v_{in}
to
v_{out}
with capacity
w(v)

2.

For each pair of vertices
(v,w)
which is an edge of multiplicity
1
in
E(\alpha)
(or part of a hyperedge of multiplicity
1
in
E(\alpha)
), we create a directed edge with infinite capacity from
v_{out}
to
w_{in}
and we create a directed edge with infinite capacity from
w_{out}
to
v_{in}
.

3.

We define
U_{H_{\alpha}}
to be
U_{H_{\alpha}}=\{u_{in}:u\in U_{\alpha}\}
and we define
V_{H_{\alpha}}
to be
V_{H_{\alpha}}=\{v_{out}:v\in V_{\alpha}\}

Lemma 9.35
.

The maximum flow from
U_{H_{\alpha}}
to
V_{H_{\alpha}}
is equal to the minimum weight of a separator between
U_{\alpha}
and
V_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

This can be proved using the max flow min cut theorem.

Definition 9.36

(Modified Flow Graph)
.

Given a shape
\alpha
together with a set
I_{L}\subseteq V(\alpha)
of vertices in
\alpha
(which will be the vertices in
\alpha
which are intersected with a vertex to the left of
\alpha
) and a set
I_{R}\subseteq V(\alpha)
of vertices in
\alpha
(which will be the vertices in
\alpha
which are intersected with a vertex to the right of
\alpha
), we define the modified flow graph
H^{I_{L},I_{R}}_{\alpha}
as follows:

1.

We start with the flow graph
H_{\alpha}

2.

For each vertex
u\in I_{L}
, we delete all of the edges into
u_{in}
and add
u_{in}
to
U_{H_{\alpha}}

3.

For each vertex
v\in I_{R}
, we delete all of the edges out of
v_{out}
and add
v_{out}
to
V_{H_{\alpha}}

4.

We call the resulting graph
H^{I_{L},I_{R}}_{\alpha}
and the resulting sets
U_{H^{I_{L},I_{R}}_{\alpha}}
and
V_{H^{I_{L},I_{R}}_{\alpha}}

Lemma 9.37
.

The maximum flow from
U_{H^{I_{L},I_{R}}_{\alpha}}
to
V_{H^{I_{L},I_{R}}_{\alpha}}
in
H^{I_{L},I_{R}}_{\alpha}
is at least as large as the maximum flow from
U_{H_{\alpha}}
to
V_{H_{\alpha}}
in
H_{\alpha}

Proof sketch.

Observe that if we have a cut
C
in
H^{I_{L},I_{R}}_{\alpha}
which separates
U_{H^{I_{L},I_{R}}_{\alpha}}
and
V_{H^{I_{L},I_{R}}_{\alpha}}
then
C
separates
U_{H_{\alpha}}
and
V_{H_{\alpha}}
in
H_{\alpha}

Before the intersections, we have the following flows.

1.

We take
F_{1}
to be the maximum flow from
U_{\gamma}
to
V_{\gamma}
in
\gamma
. Note that
F_{1}
has value
w(V_{\gamma})

2.

We take
F_{2}
to be the maximum flow from
U_{\tau}
to
V_{\tau}
in
\tau
. Note that
F_{2}
has value
w(S_{\tau,min})

3.

We take
F_{3}
to be the maximum flow from
U_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
to
V_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
in
{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
. Note that
F_{1}
has value
w(U_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}})

After the intersections, we take the following flows:

1.

We take
F^{\prime}_{1}
to be the maximum flow from
U_{H^{\emptyset,I_{LM}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR}}_{\gamma}}
to
V_{H^{\emptyset,I_{LM}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR}}_{\gamma}}
in

H^{\emptyset,I_{LM}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR}}_{\gamma}
.

2.

We take
F^{\prime}_{2}
to be the maximum flow from
U_{H^{I_{LM}\cup I_{LMR},I_{MR}\cup I_{LMR}}_{\tau}}
to
V_{H^{I_{LM}\cup I_{LMR},I_{MR}\cup I_{LMR}}_{\tau}}
in

H^{I_{LM}\cup I_{LMR},I_{MR}\cup I_{LMR}}_{\tau}

3.

We take
F^{\prime}_{3}
to be the maximum flow from
U_{H^{I_{MR}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR},\emptyset}_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}}
to
V_{H^{I_{MR}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR},\emptyset}_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}}
in

H^{I_{MR}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR},\emptyset}_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
.

Observe that because of how intersection patterns are defined,
val(F^{\prime}_{1})=w(U_{\gamma})
and
val(F^{\prime}_{3})=w(V_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}})
. By Lemma
9.37
, the value of
F^{\prime}_{2}
is at least as large as the value of
F_{2}
, so
val(F^{\prime}_{2})\geq w(S_{\tau,min})
.

We now consider
F^{\prime}_{1}+F^{\prime}_{2}+F^{\prime}_{3}
. As is, this is not a flow, but we can fix this.

Definition 9.38
.

For each vertex
v\in V(\tau_{P})
,

1.

We define
f_{in}(v)
to be the flow into
v_{in}
in
F^{\prime}_{1}+F^{\prime}_{2}+F^{\prime}_{3}
.

2.

We define
f_{out}(v)
to be the flow out of
v_{out}
in
F^{\prime}_{1}+F^{\prime}_{2}+F^{\prime}_{3}
.

3.

We define
f_{through}(v)
to be the flow from
v_{in}
to
v_{out}
in
F^{\prime}_{1}+F^{\prime}_{2}+F^{\prime}_{3}

4.

We define
f_{imbalance}(v)
to be
f_{imbalance}(v)=|f_{in}(v)-f_{out}(v)|

5.

We define
f_{excess}(v)
to be
f_{excess}(v)=f_{through}(v)-max{\{f_{in}(v),f_{out}(v)\}}

With this information, we fix the flow
F^{\prime}_{1}+F^{\prime}_{2}+F^{\prime}_{3}
as follows. For each vertex
v\in V(\tau_{P})
,

1.

If
f_{in}(v)>f_{out}(v)
then we create a vertex
v_{supplemental,out}
and an edge from
v_{out}
to
v_{supplemental,out}
with capacity
f_{imbalance}(v)
and we route
f_{imbalance}(v)
of flow along this edge. We then add
v_{supplemental,out}
to a set of vertices
V_{supplemental}
.

2.

If
f_{in}(v)<f_{out}(v)
then we create a vertex
v_{supplemental,in}
and an edge from
v_{supplemental,in}
to
v_{in}
with capacity
f_{imbalance}(v)
and we route
f_{imbalance}(v)
of flow along this edge. We then add
v_{supplemental,out}
to a set of vertices
V_{supplemental}
.

3.

We reduce the flow on the edge from
v_{in}
to
v_{out}
by
f_{excess}(v)

We call the resulting flow
F^{\prime}

Proposition 9.39
.

F^{\prime}
is a flow from
U_{H^{\emptyset,I_{LM}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR}}_{\gamma}}\cup U_{supplemental}
to
V_{H^{I_{MR}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR},\emptyset}_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}}\cup V_{supplemental}
with value
val(F^{\prime})=val(F^{\prime}_{1})+val(F^{\prime}_{2})+val(F^{\prime}_{3})-\sum_{v\in V(\tau)}{f_{excess}(v)}

Corollary 9.40
.

There exists a flow
F^{\prime\prime}
from
U_{H^{\emptyset,I_{LM}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR}}_{\gamma}}
to
V_{H^{I_{MR}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR},\emptyset}_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}}
with value
val(F^{\prime\prime})\geq val(F^{\prime}_{1})+val(F^{\prime}_{2})+val(F^{\prime}_{3})-\sum_{v\in V(\tau)}{(f_{excess}(v)+f_{imbalance}(v))}

Proof.

Consider the minimum cut
C
between
U_{H^{\emptyset,I_{LM}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR}}_{\gamma}}
and
V_{H^{I_{MR}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR},\emptyset}_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}}
. If we add all of the supplemental edges to
C
then this gives a cut
C^{\prime}
between
U_{H^{\emptyset,I_{LM}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR}}_{\gamma}}
and
V_{H^{I_{MR}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR},\emptyset}_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}}
with capacity

capacity(C^{\prime})=capacity(C)+\sum_{v\in V(\tau)}{f_{imbalance}(v)}\geq val(F^{\prime})

Thus,
capacity(C)\geq val(F^{\prime})-\sum_{v\in V(\tau)}{f_{imbalance}(v)}
so there exists a flow
F^{\prime\prime}
from
U_{H^{\emptyset,I_{LM}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR}}_{\gamma}}
to
V_{H^{I_{MR}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR},\emptyset}_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}}
with value

val(F^{\prime\prime})=capacity(C)\geq val(F^{\prime}_{1})+val(F^{\prime}_{2})+val(F^{\prime}_{3})-\sum_{v\in V(\tau)}{(f_{excess}(v)+f_{imbalance}(v))}

We now make the following observations:

Lemma 9.41
.

1.

For all vertices
v\notin I_{LM}\cup I_{MR}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR}
,
f_{excess}(v)=f_{imbalance}(v)=0
(and these vertices can never be isolated).

2.

For all vertices
v\in I_{LM}
,
f_{excess}(v)+f_{imbalance}(v)\leq w(v)
. Moreover, for all vertices
v\in I_{LM}
which are isolated,
f_{excess}(v)=f_{imbalance}(v)=0
.

3.

For all vertices
v\in I_{MR}
,
f_{excess}(v)+f_{imbalance}(v)\leq w(v)
. Moreover, for all vertices
v\in I_{LM}
which are isolated,
f_{excess}(v)=f_{imbalance}(v)=0
.

4.

For all vertices
v\in I_{LR}
,
f_{excess}(v)+f_{imbalance}(v)\leq w(v)
. Moreover, for all vertices
v\in I_{LM}
which are isolated,
f_{excess}(v)=f_{imbalance}(v)=0
.

5.

For all vertices
v\in I_{LMR}
,
f_{excess}(v)+f_{imbalance}(v)\leq 2w(v)
. Moreover, for all vertices
v\in I_{LMR}
which are isolated,
f_{excess}(v)=w(v)
and
f_{imbalance}(v)=0
.

Proof.

For the first statement, observe that for vertices
v\notin I_{LM}\cup I_{MR}\cup I_{LR}\cup I_{LMR}
, neither
v_{in}
nor
v_{out}
