such that there is a path from
V_{\alpha}
to
v
which doesn’t intersect
S_{1}\cup S_{2}
before reaching
v
.

Now observe that
S_{L}
is a vertex separator between
U_{\alpha}
and
S_{1}
. To see this, note that for any path
P
from
U_{\alpha}
to a vertex
v\in S_{1}
, either
P
intersects
S_{L}
before reaching
v
or
P
does not intersect
S_{L}
before reaching
v
. In the latter case,
v\in S_{L}
. Thus, in either case,
P
intersects
S_{L}
. Following similar logic,
S_{L}
is also a vertex separator between
U_{\alpha}
and
S_{2}
,
S_{R}
is a vertex separator between
S_{1}
and
V_{\alpha}
, and
S_{R}
is also a vertex separator between
S_{2}
and
V_{\alpha}
.

To show that
w(S_{L})+w(S_{R})\leq w(S_{1})+w(S_{2})
, observe that
w(S_{L})+w(S_{R})=w(S_{R}\cup S_{R})+w(S_{L}\cap S_{R})
and
w(S_{1})+w(S_{2})=w(S_{1}\cup S_{2})+w(S_{1}\cap S_{2})
. Thus, to show that
w(S_{L})+w(S_{R})\leq w(S_{1})+w(S_{2})
, it is sufficient to show that

1.

S_{L}\cup S_{R}\subseteq S_{1}\cup S_{2}

2.

S_{L}\cap S_{R}\subseteq S_{1}\cap S_{2}

For the first statement, note that by definition any vertex in
S_{L}\cup S_{R}
must be in
S_{1}\cup S_{2}
. For the second statement, note that if
v\in S_{L}\cap S_{R}
then there is a path from
U_{\alpha}
to
v
which does not intersect any other vertices in
S_{1}\cup S_{2}
and there is a path from
v
to
V_{\alpha}
which does not intersect any other vertices in
S_{1}\cup S_{2}
. Combining these paths, we obtain a path
P
from
U_{\alpha}
to
V_{\alpha}
such that
v
is the only vertex in
P
which is in
S_{1}\cup S_{2}
. This implies that
v\in S_{1}\cap S_{2}
as otherwise either
S_{1}
or
S_{2}
would not be a vertex separator between
U_{\alpha}
and
V_{\alpha}
.

Corollary A.2
.

The leftmost and rightmost minimum vertex separators between
U_{\alpha}
and
V_{\alpha}
are well-defined.

Proof.

Assume that there is no minimum leftmost vertex separator. If so, then there exists a minimum vertex separator
S_{1}
between
U_{\alpha}
and
V_{\alpha}
such that

1.

There does not exist a minimum vertex separator
S^{\prime}
of
\alpha
such that
S^{\prime}
is also a minimum vertex separator of
U_{\alpha}
and
S_{1}
(otherwise we would take
S^{\prime}
rather than
S
)

2.

There exists a minimum vertex separator
S_{2}
of
\alpha
such that
S^{\prime}
is not a minimum vertex separator of
U_{\alpha}
and
S_{2}
(as otherwise
S_{1}
would be the leftmost minimum vertex separator)

Now let
S_{L}
and
S_{R}
be the vertex separators of
\alpha
obtained by applying Lemma
A.1
to
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
. Since
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are minimum vertex separators of
\alpha
, we must have that
w(S_{L})=w(S_{R})=w(S_{1})=w(S_{2})
. Since
S_{L}
is a vertex separator of
U_{\alpha}
and
S_{2}
,
S_{L}\neq S_{1}
. However,
S_{L}
is a vertex separator of
U_{\alpha}
and
S_{1}
, which contradicts our choice of
S_{1}
.

Thus, there must be a leftmost minimum vertex separator of
\alpha
. Following similar logic, there must be a rightmost minimum vertex separator of
\alpha
as well.

Proofs with Canonical Maps

In this section, we give alternative proofs of Lemmas

\alpha

\varphi_{R}(V_{\alpha})=B_{R}
. Note that there are
|Aut(\alpha)|
possible choices for this map.

Lemma B.2
.

M^{orth}_{\tau}(H)=\sum_{\sigma\in Row(H),\sigma^{\prime}\in Col(H)}{H(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})|Decomp(\sigma,\tau,{\sigma^{\prime}}^{T})|M_{\sigma\circ\tau\circ{\sigma^{\prime}}^{T}}}

Proof of Lemma
8.14

Definition B.3

(Rigorous definition of intersection patterns)
.

We define an intersection pattern
P
on composable shapes
\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
to consist of the shape
\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
together with a non-empty set of constraint edges
E(P)
on
V(\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})
such that:

1.

For all vertices
u,v,w\in V(\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})
, if
(u,v),(v,w)\in E(P)
then
(u,w)\in E(P)

2.

E(P)
does not contain a path between two vertices of
\gamma
, two vertices of
\tau
, or two vertices of
{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
. This ensures that when we consider
\gamma,\tau,\gamma^{\prime}
individually, their vertices are distinct.

3.

Defining
V_{*}(\gamma)\subseteq V(\gamma)
to be the vertices of
\gamma
which are incident to an edge in
E(P)
,
U_{\gamma}
is the unique minimum-weight vertex separator between
U_{\gamma}
and
V_{*}(\gamma)\cup V_{\gamma}

4.

Similarly, defining
V_{*}({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})\subseteq V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})
to be the vertices of
{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
which are incident to an edge in
E(P)
,
V_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
is the unique minimum-weight vertex separator between
V_{*}({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})\cup U_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
and
V_{U_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}}

5.*

All edges in
E(P)
are between vertices of the same type.

Definition B.4
.

We say that two intersection patterns
P,P^{\prime}
on shapes
\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
are equivalent (which we write as
P\equiv P^{\prime}
) if there is an automorphism
\pi\in Aut(\gamma)\times Aut(\tau)\times Aut({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})
such that
\pi(P)=P^{\prime}
(i.e. if
E(P)
and
E(P^{\prime})
are the constraint edges for
P
and
P^{\prime}
respectively then
\pi(E(P))=E(P^{\prime})
).

Definition B.5
.

Given composable shapes
\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
, we define
\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
to be the set of all possible intersection patterns
P
on
\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}
(up to equivalence)

Definition B.6
.

Given composable (but not properly composable) ribbons
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
,
R_{3}
of shapes
\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}
, we define the intersection pattern
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
induced by
R_{1},R_{2},R_{3}
as follows:

1.

Take the canonical maps
\varphi_{R_{1}}:V(\gamma)\rightarrow V(R_{1})
,
\varphi_{R_{2}}:V(\tau)\rightarrow V(R_{2})
, and
\varphi_{R_{3}}:V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})\rightarrow V(R_{3})

2.

Given vertices
u\in V(\gamma)
and
v\in V(\tau)
, add a constraint edge between
u
and
v
if and only if
\varphi_{R_{1}}(u)=\varphi_{R_{2}}(v)
. Similarly, given vertices
u\in V(\gamma)
and
w\in V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})
, add a constraint edge between
u
and
w
if and only if
\varphi_{R_{1}}(u)=\varphi_{R_{3}}(w)
and given vertices
v\in V(\tau)
and
w\in V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})
, add a constraint edge between
v
and
w
if and only if
\varphi_{R_{2}}(v)=\varphi_{R_{3}}(w)
.

Definition B.7
.

Given an intersection pattern
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
, we define
V(\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})/E(P)
to be
V(\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})
where all of the edges in
E(P)
are contracted (i.e. if
(u,v)\in E(P)
then
u=v
and
u=v
only appears once).

Definition B.8
.

Given an intersection pattern
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
, we define
\tau_{P}
to be the shape such that:

1.

V(H_{\tau_{P}})=V(\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})/E(P)

2.

E(H_{\tau_{P}})=E(\gamma)\cup E(\tau)\cup E({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})

3.

U_{\tau_{P}}=U_{\gamma}

4.

V_{\tau_{P}}=V_{{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}

Definition B.9
.

Given an intersection pattern
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,\tau,{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}}
, we make the following definitions:

1.

We define
Aut(P)=\{\pi\in Aut(\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}):\pi(E(P))=E(P)\}

2.

We define
Aut_{pieces}(P)=\{\pi\in Aut(U_{\gamma})\times Aut(\tau)\times Aut({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}):\pi(E(P))=E(P)\}

3.

We define
N(P)=|Aut(P)/Aut_{pieces}(P)|

B_{R_{5}}=A_{R^{\prime}_{3}}
.

To confirm that this is a bijection, we need to show that these maps are inverses of each other.

If we apply the first map and then the second, we obtain the following:

1.

We obtain the ribbons

(a)

R^{\prime\prime}_{1}=R^{\prime}_{1}\circ\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\varphi^{-1}_{R^{\prime}_{2}}(\varphi_{R_{4}}\circ\varphi_{R_{2}}\circ\varphi_{R_{5}})(V(\gamma))

(b)

R^{\prime\prime}_{2}=\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\varphi^{-1}_{R^{\prime}_{2}}(\varphi_{R_{4}}\circ\varphi_{R_{2}}\circ\varphi_{R_{5}})(V(\tau))

(c)

R^{\prime\prime}_{3}=\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\varphi^{-1}_{R^{\prime}_{2}}(\varphi_{R_{4}}\circ\varphi_{R_{2}}\circ\varphi_{R_{5}})(V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}))\circ R^{\prime}_{3}

where

(a)

R^{\prime}_{1}
is the part of
R_{1}
between
A_{R_{1}}
and
(S^{\prime},O_{S^{\prime}})
where
S^{\prime}
is the minimum vertex separator between
A_{R_{1}}
and
V_{*}\cup B_{R_{1}}
.

(b)

R_{4}
is the part of
R_{1}
between
(S^{\prime},O_{S^{\prime}})
and
B_{R_{1}}

(c)

R^{\prime}_{2}
is the ribbon of shape
\tau_{P}
induced (along with the intersection pattern
P
) by
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
, and
R_{3}
.

(d)

R_{5}
is the part of
R_{3}
between
A_{R_{3}}
and
(T^{\prime},O_{T^{\prime}})
.

(e)

R^{\prime}_{3}
is the part of
R_{3}
between
(T^{\prime},O_{T^{\prime}})
and
B_{R_{3}}

This implies that
R^{\prime\prime}_{1}=R^{\prime}_{1}\circ R_{4}=R_{1}
,
R^{\prime\prime}_{2}=R_{2}
, and
R^{\prime\prime}_{3}=R_{5}\circ R^{\prime}_{3}=R_{3}
. Since the second map leaves
R^{\prime}_{1}
and
R^{\prime}_{3}
unchanged, we recover the orderings
O_{S}
and
O_{T}
as well.

Conversely, if we apply the second map, we have that
R_{1}=R^{\prime}_{1}\circ\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(V(\gamma))
,
R_{2}=\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(V(\tau))
, and
R_{3}=\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}))\circ R^{\prime}_{3}
and we have the orderings
O_{S}
and
O_{T}
corresponding to
B_{R^{\prime}_{1}}
and
A_{R^{\prime}_{3}}
respectively. If we apply the first map,

1.

R^{\prime}_{1}
and
R^{\prime}_{3}
are preserved.

2.

R^{\prime\prime}_{2}
and
P^{\prime\prime}
are the ribbon and intersection pattern induced by the ribbons
\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(\gamma)
,
\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(\tau)
, and
\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})
. To see that
R^{\prime\prime}_{2}=R^{\prime}_{2}
, observe that

R^{\prime\prime}_{2}=\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(V(\gamma))\circ\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(V(\tau))\circ\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}))=\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}{\pi(\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})}=\varphi_{R_{2}}(\gamma\circ\tau\circ{\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})=R^{\prime}_{2}

To see that
P^{\prime\prime}\equiv P
, observe that:

(a)

We have two bijective maps from
V(\gamma)
to
V(\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(\gamma))
. These two maps are
\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi
and
\varphi_{\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(\gamma)}
.

(b)

We have two bijective maps from
V(\tau)
to
V(\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(\tau))
. These two maps are
\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi
and
\varphi_{\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(\tau)}
.

(c)

We have two bijective maps from
V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})
to
V(\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}))
. These two maps are
\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi
and
\varphi_{\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T})}
.

(d)

For
P^{\prime\prime}
, the constraint edges are

\left(\varphi^{-1}_{\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(\gamma)}\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi\circ\varphi^{-1}_{\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(\tau)}\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi\circ\varphi^{-1}_{\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}))}\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi\right)(E(P))

3.

We have that

\pi^{\prime\prime}=\varphi^{-1}_{R^{\prime}_{2}}(\varphi_{\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(V(\gamma))}\circ\varphi_{\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(V(\tau))}\circ\varphi_{\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}))})

To see that
\pi^{\prime\prime}\equiv\pi
, note that

\pi=\pi^{\prime\prime}\left(\varphi^{-1}_{\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(V(\gamma))}\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi\circ\varphi^{-1}_{\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(V(\tau))}\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi\circ\varphi^{-1}_{\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi(V({\gamma^{\prime}}^{T}))}\varphi_{R^{\prime}_{2}}\pi)\right)

The analysis for the the first term is the same except that when
\gamma^{\prime}
is trivial, we always take
\gamma^{\prime}
to be the identity so
T=V(V_{\tau})=V(U_{{\sigma^{\prime}}^{T}})
and the ordering
O_{T}
is given by
V_{\tau}=U_{{\sigma^{\prime}}^{T}}
.
Similarly, the analysis for the the second term is the same except that when
\gamma
is trivial, we always take
\gamma
to be the identity so
S=V(V_{\sigma})=V(U_{\tau})
and the ordering
O_{S}
is given by
V_{\sigma}=U_{\tau}
.

Appendix C
Degree 4 Planted Clique Analysis

For this example, we name the shapes based on what they look like to make them easier to keep track of. With the exception of
Id_{U}
, these names only appear in this section.

C.1
The shapes
\alpha
and coefficients
\lambda_{\alpha}

After several preprocessing steps, the moment matrix which needs to be analyzed is
M\approx\sum_{\alpha}{\lambda_{\alpha}M_{\alpha}}
for the following shapes
\alpha
and coefficients
\lambda_{\alpha}

Definition C.1
.

1.

Given
E\subseteq\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{1},v_{2}),(u_{2},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}
, we define
\alpha_{E}
to be the shape where
U_{\alpha_{E}}=(u_{1},u_{2})
,
V_{\alpha_{E}}=(v_{1},v_{2})
, and
E(\alpha)=E
.

2.

Given
E\subseteq\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{1},v_{2}),(u_{2},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}
, we define
\alpha_{X,E}
to be the shape where
U_{\alpha_{E}}=(u_{1},u_{2})
,
V_{\alpha_{E}}=(v_{1},v_{2})
, there is one additional vertex
w_{1}
, and
E(\alpha)=E\cup\{(u_{1},w_{1}),(u_{2},w_{1}),(w_{1},v_{1}),(w_{1},v_{2})\}
.

3.

Given
i,j\in\{1,2\}
, we define
\alpha_{u_{i}=v_{j},e}
to be the shape where
U_{\alpha_{u_{i}=v_{j},e}}=(u_{1},u_{2})
,
V_{\alpha_{u_{i}=v_{j},e}}=(v_{1},v_{2})
,
u_{i}=v_{j}
, and
E(\alpha_{u_{i}=v_{j},e})=\{(u_{2-i},v_{2-j})\}
.

4.

Given
i,j\in\{1,2\}
, we define
\alpha_{u_{i}=v_{j},\emptyset}
to be the shape where
U_{\alpha_{E}}=(u_{1},u_{2})
,
V_{\alpha_{E}}=(v_{1},v_{2})
,
u_{i}=v_{j}
, and
E(\alpha)=\emptyset
.

5.

We define
\alpha_{Id:}=Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}
to be the shape where
U_{Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}}=V_{Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}}=(u_{1},u_{2})
and
E(Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})})=\emptyset
.

6.

We define
\alpha_{swap}
to be the shape where
U_{\alpha_{swap}}=(u_{1},u_{2})
,
V_{\alpha_{swap}}=(u_{2},u_{1})
and
E(\alpha_{swap})=\emptyset
.

For illustrations of these shapes
\alpha
, see Figures
7
.

We have the following coefficients on these shapes.

1.

For each
E\subseteq\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{1},v_{2}),(u_{2},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}
,
\lambda_{\alpha_{E}}=\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}
.

2.

For each
E\subseteq\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{1},v_{2}),(u_{2},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}
,
\lambda_{\alpha_{X,E}}=C\frac{k^{5}}{n^{5}}
for some constant
C>1
. These coefficients are the ad-hoc fix to the candidate pseudo-expectation values for planted clique in
[

]
.

3.

For each
i,j\in\{1,2\}

\lambda_{\alpha_{u_{i}=v_{j},e}}=\lambda_{\alpha_{u_{i}=v_{j},\emptyset}}=\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}
.

4.

\lambda_{\alpha_{Id:}}=\lambda_{\alpha_{swap}}=\frac{k^{2}}{n^{2}}

C.2
Decomposing
\alpha
and coefficient matrices

To find the coefficient matrices
H_{Id_{\emptyset}}
,
H_{Id_{(u_{1})}}
,
H_{Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}}
, and
H_{\tau}
, we need to decompose each
\alpha
into a left part
\sigma
, a proper middle part
\tau
, and a right part
{\sigma^{\prime}}^{T}
.

The following left shapes will appear in these decompositions

Definition C.2
.

1.

Define
\sigma_{Id:}=Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}
. Note that
\sigma_{Id:}=\alpha_{Id:}
but it is playing a different role.

2.

Define
\sigma_{swap}=\alpha_{swap}
.

3.

Define
\sigma_{7}
to be the shape where
U_{\sigma_{7}}=(u_{1},u_{2})
,
V_{\sigma_{7}}=(v_{1})
, and
E(\sigma_{7})=\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{1})\}
.

4.

Define

U_{\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}}=(u_{1},u_{2})
,
V_{\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}}=(u_{1})
, and
E(\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}})=\emptyset
.

5.

Similarly, define
\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}
to be the shape where
U_{\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}}=(u_{1},u_{2})
,
V_{\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}}=(u_{2})
, and
E(\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}})=\emptyset
.

6.

Define
\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}
to be the shape where
U_{\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}}=(u_{1},u_{2})
,
V_{\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}}=\emptyset
, and
E(\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset})=\emptyset
.

The following proper middle shapes will appear in these decompositions.

Definition C.3
.

1.

Define
\tau_{Id:}=Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}

2.

Given
E\subseteq\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{1},v_{2}),(u_{2},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}
such that all four vertices
u_{1},u_{2},v_{1},v_{2}
are incident to at least one edge in
E

\tau_{E}=\alpha_{E}
.

3.

Given
E\subseteq\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{1},v_{2}),(u_{2},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}
such that

, we define
\tau_{X,E}=\alpha_{X,E}
.

4.

Given
i,j\in\{1,2\}
, we define
\tau_{u_{i}=v_{j},e}=\alpha_{u_{i}=v_{j},e}
.

5.

Define
\tau_{Id\cdot}=Id_{(u_{1})}
to be the shape where
U_{Id_{(u_{1})}}=V_{Id_{(u_{1})}}=(u_{1})
and
E(Id_{(u_{1})})=\emptyset
.

6.

Define
\tau_{e}
to be the shape where
U_{\tau_{e}}=(u_{1})
,
V_{\tau_{e}}=(v_{1})
, and
E(\tau_{e})=\{(u_{1},v_{1})\}
.

7.

Define
\tau_{\emptyset}
to be the empty shape with no vertices.

These proper middle shapes (except for
\tau_{\emptyset}
) are illustrated in Figure

.

Some decompositions are as follows:

Example C.4
.

1.

\alpha_{X,\emptyset}=\sigma_{7}\circ\tau_{Id\cdot}\circ\sigma_{7}^{T}
(see Figure
14
).

2.

\alpha_{\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{1})\}}=\sigma_{7}\circ\tau_{Id\cdot}\circ\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}^{T}
(see Figure
15
).

3.

\alpha_{\{(u_{2},v_{1})\}}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}\circ\tau_{e}\circ\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}^{T}
(see Figure
16
).

4.

\displaystyle\alpha_{\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{Id:}\circ\alpha_{\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}}\circ\sigma_{Id:}^{T}=\sigma_{Id:}\circ\alpha_{\{(u_{1},v_{2}),(u_{2},v_{1})\}}\circ\sigma_{swap}^{T}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{swap}^{T}\circ\alpha_{\{(u_{1},v_{2}),(u_{2},v_{1})\}}\circ\sigma_{Id:}=\sigma_{swap}\circ\alpha_{\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}}\circ\sigma_{swap}^{T}

Remark C.5
.

Since there are
4
different ways to decompose
\alpha_{\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}}
, we split the coefficient
\lambda_{\alpha_{\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}}}
among these four decompositions. This is the reason for the factor of
4
in the denominator in the entries of the matrix
H_{\tau}
.

This figure shows the decomposition

.

This figure shows the decomposition

.

Figure 16:
This figure shows the decomposition

.

Our coefficient matrices are as follows (ignoring zero rows and columns):

1.

H_{Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}}
has two rows and columns indexed by
\sigma_{Id:}
and
\sigma_{swap}
and has entries
\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{2}}{2n^{2}}&\frac{k^{2}}{2n^{2}}\\
\frac{k^{2}}{2n^{2}}&\frac{k^{2}}{2n^{2}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)
.

2.

H_{Id_{(u_{1})}}
has rows and columns indexed by
\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}
,
\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}
, and
\sigma_{7}
and has entries

\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}\\
\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}\\
\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&C\frac{k^{5}}{n^{5}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)

3.

H_{Id_{\emptyset}}
has a single row and column indexed by
\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}
and has a single entry which is
\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}
.

4.

For all
E\subseteq\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{1},v_{2}),(u_{2},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}
such that all four vertices
u_{1},u_{2},v_{1},v_{2}
are incident to at least one edge in
E
,
H_{\tau_{E}}
has two rows and columns indexed by
\sigma_{Id:}
and
\sigma_{swap}
and has entries
\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{4}}{4n^{4}}&\frac{k^{4}}{4n^{4}}\\
\frac{k^{4}}{4n^{4}}&\frac{k^{4}}{4n^{4}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)
.

5.

For all
E\subseteq\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{1},v_{2}),(u_{2},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}
such that
E\neq\emptyset
,
H_{\tau_{X,E}}
has two rows and columns indexed by
\sigma_{Id:}
and
\sigma_{swap}
and has entries
\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{5}}{4n^{5}}&\frac{k^{5}}{4n^{5}}\\
\frac{k^{5}}{4n^{5}}&\frac{k^{5}}{4n^{5}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)
.

6.

For all
i,j\in\{1,2\}
,
H_{\tau_{u_{i}=v_{j},e}}
has two rows and columns indexed by
\sigma_{Id:}
and
\sigma_{swap}
and has entries
\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{3}}{4n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{4n^{3}}\\
\frac{k^{3}}{4n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{4n^{3}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)
.

7.

H_{\tau_{e}}
has rows and columns indexed by
\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}
and
\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}
and has entries

\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}\\
\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)

C.3
Verifying the first and second conditions of the machinery

We can verify the fist and second conditions of the machinery as follows.

1.

H_{Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}}\succeq 0
and
H_{Id_{\emptyset}}\succeq 0

2.

As long as
C\geq 1
,
H_{Id_{(u_{1})}}\succeq 0
. This condition is the reason why we need to add this term in.

3.

For all
E\subseteq\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{1},v_{2}),(u_{2},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}
such that all four vertices
u_{1},u_{2},v_{1},v_{2}
are incident to at least one edge in
E
,
||M_{\tau_{E}}||
is
\tilde{O}(n)
so
||M_{\tau_{E}}||H_{\tau_{E}}\preceq H_{Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}}
as long as
k<<\sqrt{n}
.

4.

For all
E\subseteq\{(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{1},v_{2}),(u_{2},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\}
such that
E\neq\emptyset
,

||M_{\tau_{X,E}}||
is
\tilde{O}(n^{3/2})
so
||M_{\tau_{X,E}}||H_{\tau_{X,E}}\preceq H_{Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}}
as long as
k<<\sqrt{n}
.

5.

For all
i,j\in\{1,2\}
,
||M_{\tau_{u_{i}=v_{j},e}}||
is
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{n})
so
||M_{\tau_{u_{i}=v_{j},e}}||H_{\tau_{u_{i}=v_{j},e}}\preceq H_{Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}}
as long as
k<<\sqrt{n}
.

6.

Since
\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}\\
\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}\\
\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&C\frac{k^{5}}{n^{5}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)\succeq(1-\frac{1}{C})\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&0\\
\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&0\\
0&0&0\\
\end{matrix}\right)
and
||M_{\tau_{e}}||
is
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{n})
,
||M_{\tau_{e}}||H_{\tau_{e}}\preceq H_{Id_{(u_{1})}}
as long as
C>1
and
k<<\sqrt{n}
. Note that for pseudo-calibration we take
C=1
. We can do this because we have more terms which allows us to have a more delicate factorization.

C.4
Verifying the third condition of the machinery

The following left shapes
\gamma
appear.

Definition C.6
.

1.

Define
\gamma_{7}=\sigma_{7}
.

2.

Define
\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}
.

3.

Define
\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}
.

4.

Define
\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}
.

5.

Define
\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset}
to be the shape with
U_{\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset}}=(u_{1})
and
V_{\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset}}=\emptyset

For illustrations of these gammas, see Figure
17
.

Figure 17:
This figure shows the left shapes
\gamma
which appear in the analysis. From left to right we have
\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}
,
\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}
,
\gamma_{7}
,
\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}
, and
\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset}

We have the following compositions.

1.

\sigma_{Id:}\circ\gamma_{7}=\sigma_{swap}\circ\gamma_{7}=\sigma_{7}
(see Figure
18
).

2.

\sigma_{Id:}\circ\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}
and
\sigma_{swap}\circ\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}
(see Figure
19
).

3.

Similarly,
\sigma_{Id:}\circ\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}
and
\sigma_{swap}\circ\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}
.

4.

\sigma_{Id:}\circ\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}=\sigma_{swap}\circ\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}
.

5.

\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}\circ\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}\circ\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}
(see Figure
20
).

Figure 18:
This figure shows the compositions
\sigma_{Id:}\circ\gamma_{7}=\sigma_{7}
and
\sigma_{swap}\circ\gamma_{7}=\sigma_{7}
.

Figure 19:
This figure shows the compositions
\sigma_{Id:}\circ\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}
and
\sigma_{swap}\circ\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}
.

Figure 20:
This figure shows the compositions
\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}\circ\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}
and
\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}\circ\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset}=\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}
.

Based on these compositions, we have the following matrices:

1.

H_{Id_{(u_{1})}}^{-\gamma_{7},\gamma_{7}}
has two rows and columns indexed by
\sigma_{Id:}
and
\sigma_{swap}
and has entries
\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{5}}{n^{5}}&\frac{k^{5}}{n^{5}}\\
\frac{k^{5}}{n^{5}}&\frac{k^{5}}{n^{5}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)
.

2.

H_{Id_{(u_{1})}}^{-\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}},\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}}
has two rows and columns indexed by
\sigma_{Id:}
and
\sigma_{swap}
and has entries
\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}\\
\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)
.

3.

H_{Id_{(u_{1})}}^{-\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}},\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}}=H_{Id_{(u_{1})}}^{-\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}},\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}}
.

4.

H_{Id_{\emptyset}}^{-\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset},\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}}
has two rows and columns indexed by
\sigma_{Id:}
and
\sigma_{swap}
and has entries
\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}\\
\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)
.

5.

H_{Id_{\emptyset}}^{-\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset},\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset}}
has two rows and columns indexed by
\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}
and
\sigma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}
and has entries
\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}\\
\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)
.

We can qualitatively verify the third condition of the machinery as follows

1.

B(\gamma_{7})
is
\tilde{O}(n^{\frac{|V(\gamma_{7})\setminus U_{\gamma_{7}}|}{2}})=\tilde{O}(\sqrt{n})
so
{B(\gamma_{7})^{2}}H_{Id_{(u_{1})}}^{-\gamma_{7},\gamma_{7}}\preceq H_{Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}}
as long as
k<<n^{\frac{2}{3}}
.

2.

B(\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}})
is
\tilde{O}(n^{\frac{|V(\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}})\setminus U_{\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}}|}{2}})=\tilde{O}(1)
so
{B(\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}})^{2}}H_{Id_{(u_{1})}}^{-\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}},\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{1}}}\preceq H_{Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}}
. Following the same logic,
{B(\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}})^{2}}H_{Id_{(u_{1})}}^{-\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}},\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow u_{2}}}\preceq H_{Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}}
.

3.

B(\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset})
is
\tilde{O}(n^{\frac{|V(\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset})\setminus U_{\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}}|}{2}})=\tilde{O}(1)
so
{B(\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset})^{2}}H_{Id_{\emptyset}}^{-\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset},\gamma_{u_{1},u_{2}\rightarrow\emptyset}}\preceq H_{Id_{(u_{1},u_{2})}}
.

4.

B(\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset})
is
\tilde{O}(n^{\frac{|V(\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset})\setminus U_{\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset}}|}{2}})=\tilde{O}(1)
so
{B(\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset})^{2}}H_{Id_{\emptyset}}^{-\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset},\gamma_{u_{1}\rightarrow\emptyset}}\preceq H_{Id_{(u_{1})}}
as

\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}\\
\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}\\
\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&C\frac{k^{5}}{n^{5}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)\succeq(1-\frac{1}{C})\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&0\\
\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&\frac{k^{3}}{n^{3}}&0\\
0&0&0\\
\end{matrix}\right)\succeq\tilde{O}(1)\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&0\\
\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&\frac{k^{4}}{n^{4}}&0\\
0&0&0\\
\end{matrix}\right)

.
